# Qatar: research notes

Research date 7 Nov 2026. Fact ids like [W0012] point to rows in facts.csv. The doc states each rule once; conflicts between sources, research narration and facts left out of the doc are recorded here.

## Moved out of the doc during the restructure

Conflicts, research narration and facts cut as detail when the doc was rewritten to the repo format, and in the two fix rounds that followed (7-8 Nov 2026).

### Intro

- Removed the "main bodies" list (MOI, MOL, QVC, BMET, OEP); each is now explained where it first appears in Background [W0052, W0074, W0023, W0071, S0030, L0213, W0058, M0027, M0006, B0049, B0004, B0001]. W0071 (MOL authenticates contracts) and W0074's "issues residence permits" are used only in steps now; Background keeps W0074 for the residence permit.
- Removed the old legend's "possibly outdated" reference-list explanation, the "unofficial, not verified" label and the SOP pointer explanation.

### Background: conflicts moved out

- BMET clearances for 2023 and 2024: OEP's country-clearance report gives 56,178 (2023, 5th) and 74,465 (2024, 3rd) [B0129, B0130]; BMET's annual table gives 56,148 and 74,422 [B0115]. Neither source explains the difference. The doc uses the annual table for the 2014-2024 series. OEP holds only part of 2022 (system start) [B0114].
- The Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare's post of 18 May 2026 prints 107,598 workers for "2023" [M0062]; BMET's data give 107,598 for 2025 [W0001, B0117]. The doc treats the Ministry's figure as 2025.
- Visa and medical centres: the Ministry's post of 18 May 2026 says there is only one, in Dhaka [M0063, W0009]; QVC's visa-centre page lists Dhaka and Sylhet [M0013] and its undated updates page says Sylhet takes appointments [M0014]. Left to the QVC step (work-visa writer); Background avoids naming centres.
- Maximum migration cost for Qatar: news gives Tk 100,780 (fixed for 16 countries, reported in Jan 2018 [W0104]; the Dec 2025 article dates it to 2016 [A0006]); a Joypurhat district employment office assistant director said Tk 1.15-1.2 lakh for six Gulf countries (bdnews24, July 2025) [A0011]. Kept in the doc as one Need to know line.
- Qatar-Oman Joint Tourist Visa: MOI's page names 6 countries [V0069], Visit Qatar's page about 37 [V0009]; Bangladesh is on neither. Moved out of the table.
- 72-hour business visa: its own MOI page names no nationalities [V0088], while MOI's special on-arrival page ties it to Iran [V0084]. Table now says only that it is among the special on-arrival visas for other nationalities.

### Background: research narration moved out

- The ILO pilot brief does not name the companies; only a Daily Star report of an ILO Qatar officer at a 2022 roundtable names QDVC, STS and Bonanza Overseas [W0013, D0031, G0005].
- The April 2024 labour MoU's text is not online; only a Peninsula report of the signing was found [W0011].
- Qatar's Joint Committee trades: "the Ministry's post does not say" which five TTCs [M0060]; news reports W0007, W0008 say the same [W0007, W0008].
- BOESL notice page searched for কাতার and Qatar on 7 Nov 2026, nothing found [W0006].
- BMET's miscellaneous-information page: Bangla search for কাতার returns one permission letter (BTS Overseas RL-2434, published 24 Sep 2025); English "Qatar" search returns nothing [W0004, W0005]. The letter's details (memo number, demand ref LW-QA-456) stay with the work-visa steps [W0038, W0039].
- The Ministry's Oct 2025 statements (Qatar not closed; no free visa) are known only from news reports (Financial Express, BSS); the statement itself was not found on probashi.gov.bd [W0002, W0003, B0164, B0165].
- Past restrictions (2017 slowdown, 2020 visa pause) are known only from news reports; no official record of either was found [B0166, B0167].
- Old "Need to know: a current official figure in taka for what Qatar-bound workers pay; the latest official statistic is BBS 2020's 18.1% of a year's income" [A0004]: A0004 kept as a fact; the gap note dropped from the doc.
- Old "Need to know: annual remittance figures from Qatar for FY2023-24 onwards; Bangladesh Bank's country table ends in April 2024" [B0121]: dropped from the doc (does not change what a migrant does). Open question below.
- Qatar's labour-force table (GLMM, citing the National Planning Council) splits only Qataris and non-Qataris, not by nationality [B0163].

### Background: facts cut as fluff or duplicates

- Bangladesh's minister (May 2026): more than 400,000 Bangladeshi workers in Qatar [M0061]; duplicate of Qatar's minister's 473,000 [M0067, W0010].
- Ministry (Oct 2025, news): 425,681 Bangladeshis working in Qatar and 111,662 arrivals Sept 2024-Sept 2025 [B0164]; older duplicate figures.
- Monthly remittance split July/Aug/Sept 2026: USD 121.59m, 113.09m, 127.85m (all countries USD 8,592.32m) [B0118]; doc keeps the quarter total.
- 2004-2013 yearly departures (2005 2,114; 2006 7,691; 2007 15,130; 2008 24,548; 2009 11,672; 2010 12,085; 2011 13,111; 2012 28,801; 2013 57,584) and 2025 to May 40,308 [B0115]; doc keeps 2004 and the 2014-2026 table.
- B0167 detail: 70,440 departures Jan-Oct 2017 against 102,073 a year earlier; Qatar was then focusing on skilled workers [B0167].
- B0166 detail: Qatar wanted security and hospitality workers for the 2022 World Cup [B0166].
- W0002/B0164 date: Ministry statement of 21 Oct 2025.
- Not used (Background-relevant facts not cited in the old doc, considered): W0012 (embassy: since 1976, half a million; duplicate of B0156/B0157); W0007, W0008, W0010, W0106 (news duplicates of the Ministry post M0059-M0067); W0100 (Guardian: fees illegal in Qatar; its claim's USD 1.5-2 billion figure is not in the saved text, so not used); A0002 (BBS 2023: 42.09% paid private agencies, 3.09% government); A0005 (BBS 2020: Tk 416,789 average to go abroad, all destinations); A0008 (list of cost items migrants pay beyond official fees); B0113 (2.5% remittance incentive continues; news, belongs in "Sending money home").
- Newly used in the doc from facts.csv (not cited in the old doc's Background sources): W0101 (QVC did little to curb fees), A0014 (TIB: USD 2.1 billion for visas in 2016, 90% bought), A0003 (58.24% borrowed), C0037 (Permanent Committee for Recruitment approves nationality and number; cited elsewhere in the old doc), V0125 and H0040 (early-2026 Gulf war flight suspensions; cited elsewhere in the old doc).
- The asylum sentence in the old work-visa lead (Qatar grants political asylum only to five categories [S0079, S0080, S0081, S0082, S0083]) is not Background material; left for the work-visa and asylum writers.
- Old step-level material in lines 58-124 (register and train, check the agency, check the job and employer, the crimes list under the 2013 Act, the TTC-bribe warning, minimum wage, contract form, legal aid, complaint routes, paying by bank) is left for the work-visa writer. Background repeats only the big-picture parts: fee law [W0019, L0220, W0020, W0021, A0018], the BMET letter's employer-pays condition [W0040, W0043], sub-agent registration [A0025-A0028], visa-trading law and practice [W0031-W0033, W0080-W0082, A0022, G0001], and real costs.

- Whether any nationality quota applies to Bangladeshis (MOL's "nationalities selection system") [W0023, C0037].

- Whether the ILO pilot still runs; which agencies take part [W0013, D0031].
- Any G2G channel; BOESL has no Qatar notice [W0006].
- The current government-fixed maximum migration cost for Qatar [A0018, W0104, A0006, A0011].
- An official count of workers in Qatar by nationality [B0163].
- Remittances from Qatar for FY2023-24 onwards [B0121].
- An official record of the 2017 and 2020 restrictions [B0166, B0167].

### Lead paragraph (routes)

- Moved to Background (big picture): the recruitment-route bullets were turned into one "How a job reaches you" paragraph; the asylum line ("Asylum is not a work route", five categories) was cut here because the Asylum section covers it [S0079, S0080, S0081, S0082, S0083].
- Research narration cut: BOESL's notice page had no Qatar notice "in English or Bangla" (kept only as "no Qatar job notice as of 7 Nov 2026") [W0006]; "the ILO brief does not name the companies" (the names are now attributed to the ILO officer) [D0031, G0005].
- Fluff cut: the pilot's "about 93% of workers interviewed after the pilot reported having no debt" (kept only the 92% cost cut) [W0014 still cited for the pilot].

### Step 1: Find a real job

- Facts cut as fluff / moved to Background (costs and visa trading are the Background writer's): Guardian/TBS USD 3,000-4,000 for Qatar jobs [W0103]; BBS 2020 average cost Tk 4.02 lakh [B0170]; 78% of costs go to middlemen [A0007]; traded-visa money paid outside banks [A0013]; recruitment cost 18.1% of a year's Qatar income (BBS 2020, the latest official figure) [A0004]; Qatari employers/agents demand USD 300-500 per visa, agencies buy visas for QR 5,000-6,000 [W0098, W0099]; MOI arrested nine people for fake visa-trading companies in 2023 [W0097]; OKUP 2025: free visas cost more and workers also pay work permits and job search [A0009, A0010]; Ministry Oct 2025: no free-visa category in Qatar [W0003, B0165]; Embassy FAQ's "3 years and QR 50,000" duplicates the residence law penalty [W0081].
- Kept in the doc only as worker-facing examples: workers' 2023 reports of Tk 3-4.2 lakh and fake contract letters [A0015, A0016, A0017].
- Statistic cut: OEP agency list counts on 7 Nov 2026 (2,863 agencies: 2,204 Active, 264 Expired, 235 Cancelled, 151 Suspended, 9 Server Locked) [W0087, U0002]; U0002 still cited for the list of status values.
- Research narration cut: "the one BMET permission letter seen for Qatar" (rephrased as BMET's rule [W0041]); "an English search for 'Qatar' found nothing" (kept as a how-to tip: search in Bangla) [W0005]; "the Embassy's attestation pages are from 2021" [W0046]; "the [Company Inquiry Service] page could not be reached" [A0044]; "the Ministry's post does not say [which TTCs]" [M0060].
- Interpretation not in the doc: that "LW" in "LW-QA-456" means the Embassy labour wing is our inference, not stated in the letter (facts.csv note on W0039).
- Conflict kept as one Need to know (it changes what a worker pays): the maximum migration cost for Qatar: Tk 100,780 (news 2018 [W0104] and Dhaka Tribune Dec 2025, dated 2016 in the article [A0006]) vs Tk 1.15-1.2 lakh for six Gulf countries (district employment officer, bdnews24 July 2025 [A0011]). No official order found.
- Minor change: old doc said "most migrants pay brokers" [A0001]; A0001 says about 52%, so the doc now says "about half".
- Added SOP 12's "compare with the law" list (hours, overtime, leave, probation, 5-year limit) to Step 1 item 4 [L0024, L0028, L0034, L0002, L0226], since that is where the worker first sees the terms; the "Your rights at work" writer may cover the same rules in full, so the lead may want to cut one.
- SOP 2 domestic-worker content not folded here (belongs to the domestic-worker section): 21 approved agencies for women domestic workers, no migration cost [E0037, E0038, B0030]; MOL's Sept 2026 list of 221 registered recruitment companies [E0083, A0036, W0105]; Moawen platform, moawen.mol.gov.qa [E0081, E0082]; MOL's 2023 hotline 40288101 for domestic-worker recruitment offices and its closure of 23 offices in March 2023 [A0033, A0032].
- SOP 3 domestic-worker content not folded here: housemaid visa attestation at the Embassy through an enlisted agent, its papers, QR 50, 3 working days [E0041, E0042].
- SOP 10: "Under the law, getting or selling a visa or demand letter illegally is 2-7 years" is now stated once in "A free visa is not a visa" [A0022].

- Conflict kept as one Need to know (fee): police clearance Tk 1,500 on the PCC portal (code 1-7301-0001-2681) [B0043] vs Tk 500 in Bangladesh Police's guidance (code 1-2201-0001-2681, undated) [B0047] and the Embassy in Doha's page (code 1-2201-001-2681, 2021) [B0150]. The challan codes for the Tk 500 version are left out of the doc.

### Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance, insurance and the flight

- Conflict moved out (official vs official; settled): welfare fee Tk 5,500 from 1 Oct 2026 by the Ministry's circular of 20 Sep 2026 [B0055] vs Tk 3,500 in BMET's September 2026 charter [B0051]. The circular is newer and is the rule; B0051 is still cited for the other fees.
- Conflict moved out (doesn't change what a worker does): the insurance start date: the Welfare Board's notice says workers going from 1 Jan 2023 [B0058], BMET's leaflet 12 Mar 2023 [B0059]; both give 5 years and Tk 10 lakh. JBC's contract is dated 12 Mar 2023, effective 1 Jan 2023 [H0049].
- Not folded here (domestic-worker section): women going for domestic work take a housekeeping course at one of 41 TTCs (1 month, residential, age 25-45) [B0006, E0028, E0030, E0034]; Need to know whether it replaces the 3-day PDO [B0006]. The domestic-worker items in BMET's clearance list (proof of age 25-45, guardian's no-objection letter) [B0050] are also left for that section.
- Statistic kept briefly: airport desks stopped 1,098 travellers in 2023-24 [B0064] (shows the check is real). Cut: they checked 1,391,988 smart cards that year [B0064].
- B0162 narration ("the Ministry's notices list shows no newer circular") is cited only behind the Need to know on the insurance premium.

### Step 7: After arrival

- Conflict moved out (official vs news): a 2026 NGO report says QVC-tested workers don't usually take another medical on arrival [D0053]; MOI's rule (medical within 30 days) is stated in the doc [W0078, W0076]. Open question: whether QVC-tested workers repeat any tests in Qatar.
- Conflict kept as one Need to know (who pays the late RP fine): the law puts RP renewal on the sponsor [L0100, L0121]; MOI's page says the expatriate renews and is fined QR 10 a day up to QR 6,000 [W0079].
- Fluff cut: the 2023 Gulf Times report that the CRA encouraged digital SIM registration (QID for residents, passport for visitors) [X0044; still cited behind the CRA Need to know]. Research narration cut: "cra.gov.qa was not reached"; "the QCB circular was not found on qcb.gov.qa" [X0048].
- Not folded here (family, student and domestic-worker sections): SOP 11's notes on family members (RP within 30 days, fine from day 31 [S0042]; RP QR 400 for family [E0066]); Qatar University students' RP under QU sponsorship and its Need to know [S0007, S0021, S0009, S0012]; domestic workers' RP and health card (QR 50, sponsor's QID) [E0002, U0010, U0011, U0012].
- SOP 15 (join the Welfare Board from Qatar) was not assigned to this part; Step 7 item 9 gives a short version with the Welfare Board's Oct 2026 fee (taka equivalent of Tk 6,500) [B0065]. The Embassy's older figures (QR 140 Nov 2025 [B0138], QR 170 in 2021 [B0141]), the Embassy address [B0136], hours and the membership card check sit with whoever folds SOP 15 (old doc: "If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job"). The lead may want the full steps in one place and a pointer here.
- Uncited gloss: "Hamad Medical Corporation (Qatar's main public hospital group)" and "WPS, the system that tracks salary transfers" are explanations of names, not facts in facts.csv; check with the lead or cut the gloss.
- Cross-references to other writers' sections: Step 7 item 4 points to "If you aren't paid" (old SOP 13).

### Your rights at work

- Narration cut: the Labour Law sentence said Law 14 of 2004 is "shown in force on Al Meezan, the Ministry of Justice's legal portal" [L0019].
- Moved out as covered in Step 1 of the company work visa: some professions need a training and test certificate from an MOL-accredited centre, list on MOL's website [L0073].
- Fluff cut: the US TIP report (2025) says inspectors found 5,035 companies in 2024 failing to give suitable living conditions [F0065].
- Fluff cut (opinion, no action): HRW (2026) says the WBGT 32.1 limit is set too high to protect workers [F0026].
- Narration cut: the health insurance Need to know said "no Ministry of Public Health (MoPH) notice on it was found" [R0013].
- Narration cut: the wage Need to know said "the only official figures found are the minimum wage above and the one BMET permission letter" [L0074, W0040].
- Conflict (official vs official, Welfare Board fee from Qatar): WEWB's rule (Oct 2026) is the equivalent of Tk 6,500 in local currency, life insurance included [B0065]; the welfare fee itself rose to Tk 5,500 from 1 Oct 2026 [B0055]; the Embassy's own figures are QR 140 (Nov 2025 page) [B0138] and QR 170 (2021 FAQ) [B0141]. Kept in the doc as one Need to know.
- Conflict (official vs official, where to apply for Welfare Board membership): WEWB says portal.wewb.gov.bd [B0065]; the Embassy page says www.wewb.gov.bd [B0138]. The doc uses the WEWB address.
- Conflict (official vs official, Embassy counter hours): the office-hours page says applications are taken Sunday-Thursday 08:30-13:00 and 13:30-15:30 [B0137]; the travel-permit and birth-registration pages say 08:30-16:00 [L0156, S0106]. The doc uses the office-hours page.
- Source note: the WEWB complaints focal person's details come from the 2023-24 annual report, which also lists a mobile 01817-506712 [B0094].

### How long you can stay

- Narration cut: "the residence regulations (MOI Decision 25 of 2019, in force per Al Meezan)"; "MOI's undated service page says renewal is done at..." [C0032, L0139].
- Conflict (official vs official, who receives an employer's request for prior exit approval for up to 5% of staff): the law (Law 21 of 2015) says MOL [L0102]; the Government Communications Office (GCO, March 2026) says MOI [J0001, Y0003]. The doc states the rule without naming the ministry, since the worker does not file it.
- Source date cut: the Exit Grievances Committee being free and with translation comes from a 2016 government statement [L0219].
- Conflict (law vs news, days to leave after the RP ends): the law says 90 days [L0111, S0034]; an MOI officer said in June 2026 that people must leave within 14 days of cancellation [H0037]. Stated once, as a Need to know, in Leaving at the end of the job.
- Metrash naming: MOI's older pages and some sources say Metrash2; a guide says the new Metrash app replaced Metrash2, discontinued on 1 March 2025 [M0049, L0147]. The doc says "Metrash" throughout after explaining this once.

### What can get you jailed or deported

- Narration cut: Penal Code and Cybercrime Law "both shown in force on Al Meezan on 7 Nov 2026" [X0001, X0016].
- Two official laws, different maximum fines for illegal entry or exit: Penal Code Art. 139 bis (Law 14 of 2024) up to 3 years and/or QR 100,000 [X0012]; residence law up to 3 years and/or QR 50,000 [L0114]. The doc gives the Penal Code figure under illegal entry and the residence-law figure under the residence-law offences; no Need to know, as it doesn't change what a worker does.
- Need to know moved out (doesn't change action; the doc already says never carry a packet [B0012] and that transporting drugs is punished without intent [L0127]): an official MOI warning about carrying parcels for other people.
- Embassy FAQ (2021) on the absconding fine and ban [L0174] is now written once, under If your employer reports you absent; the jailed section points to it.

- Need to know cut as covered elsewhere: the Fund's website "could not be reached" [L0094]; the Need to know itself stays.

### If you want to change employer

- Conflict moved out (outdated official pages vs law): an old MOI "Sponsorship Change" page still asks for two years with the same sponsor, an NOC and fees of QR 2,000 to 3,000 for companies (QR 1,000 to 2,000 for individuals), rising with each change [L0146]; a 2021 Hukoomi page "Apply to Replace Employer (Before Getting Residency Permit)" still asks for an NOC, QR 300 and three months since entry [R0004]. Both state the pre-2020 rules; the law (MOI Decision 51/2020) is the rule [L0122, R0001].
- Narration cut: "How to apply (reported): news reports say..." and "Check first (reported)"; the steps stay with their citations [L0185-L0190].
- Narration cut: "MOL's service pages could not be reached" from the Need to know [L0186, R0001].
- Source dates cut: HRW 2026 and 2023 [F0021, L0205]; Migrant-Rights.org 2023 [F0037]; Vialto June 2026 [H0043].

### If your employer reports you absent ("absconding")

- Narration cut from the Need to know: "the only official steps found are the Embassy's 2021 FAQ" [L0173]. The contest steps come from the Embassy's 2021 FAQ [L0173, K0003]; the Need to know on the current official way to contest a report was folded into the doc's single remaining Need to know (how to check whether a report was filed).
- Source dates cut: the government statement that employers can't file absconding after a complaint is from 2016 [L0217]; the MOL official's statement on retaliation is from 2020 [L0224]; Migrant-Rights.org 2023 [F0033, F0035, F0036]; HRW 2026 [F0023].
- Not repeated here: the 2020 MOL official's statement that employers cannot file absconding cases in retaliation against workers who complain [L0224] (the doc uses L0224 under change employer for the related point that a prior absconding case stops a change).
- Lost-passport route (old SOP 17) is in Going home without valid papers; this section points there.

### Leaving at the end of the job

- Narration cut: "the law (Law 21 of 2015, read on Al Meezan on 7 November 2026) ... and this is the rule"; "no MOI decision or law change saying this was found" [L0111, H0037, H0006].
- Conflict kept as one Need to know: 90 days in law [L0111, S0034] vs 14 days said by an MOI Airport Passports Department officer at a June 2026 webinar (earlier 30 days), then QR 10 a day [H0037, H0006]; Vialto (June 2026) says the QR 10 a day must be paid before departure and staying past 14 days may lead to a blacklist [H0043].
- Narration cut: "the decision only creates the committee, and no later decision was found on Al Meezan" (gratuity savings scheme) [X0025, X0026]; "the Customs authority's own traveller page was not found, and the English text of the Regulations on Al Meezan is a machine translation" [X0022].
- Conflict moved to Coming home (official vs official, Bangladesh currency declaration threshold): Bangladesh Bank FE Circular 06 (2020) says declare foreign currency above USD 10,000 on the FMJ form [X0059]; Bangladesh Customs' own page still says USD 5,000 [X0060]. The doc here points to Coming home without the figures.

### Work visa: domestic worker

- Research narration moved out: Moawen and MOL's list of 221 licensed offices are known only from news reports (Qatar Living, Zawya, Peninsula); mol.gov.qa could not be reached, and the official list could not be downloaded (the archived copy is damaged) [E0081, E0083, A0036, W0105].

- Research narration moved out: the Embassy's absconding guidance (Niyabah court, QR 12,000 fine) is on its 2021 FAQ page [L0173, L0174].

### Family residence (spouse and children of a worker)

- Conflict moved out: health insurance. MOI's Family Visa form (2026) requires insurance for the whole stay [E0064], and Law 22/2021 says no residence permit is granted without proof of compulsory health insurance [R0013, R0014]; Doha Guides says no health insurance (or return ticket) is needed to travel on a family residence visa [N0044]. The doc states the official rule.
- Conflict moved out: RP (QID) fee for an accompanying family member. MOI: QR 400 [E0066]; Doha Guides: QR 500 for the first year plus a QR 100 medical [N0046]. The doc states MOI's QR 400 (and the QR 100 Medical Commission fee from Hukoomi [W0077]).
- Conflict moved out: how long the visa can be used. Residence regulations Art 14: visas issued in advance are valid for use for 90 days [R0027]; Doha Guides: family members usually must enter within 180 days of approval (some recent visas 90 days or one year), and the entry visa can be renewed for QR 200 [N0045]. The doc states the regulation.

- Research narration moved out: no source was found on family-visa scams (fake or bought marriage or birth certificates, agents selling family visas) [E0071].

### Tourist visa: Hayya A1

- SOP 19 ("Apply for a Hayya Tourist Visa (A1) yourself") and SOP 20 ("Buy visitor health insurance") are folded into Step 1 (documents, insurance) and Step 2 (apply on Hayya). Hayya's own "Track your E-Visa" check (from old SOP 4, Hayya part) is written out in Step 3; the MOI Visa Inquiry how-to is referred to "Step 5 of the company work visa". The old Requirements, Cost, Processing time and "If you are refused" subsections are merged into the steps and the intro, each rule stated once.
- Conflict moved out (kept as one Need to know at Step 1): passport validity. Hayya asks 3 months from arrival [V0011, V0050, V0056]; the residence regulations (MOI Decision 25/2019, Arts 3 and 13) and MOI's visa-conditions page ask 6 months from the visa date, waivable [R0005, R0006, V0096]. The old doc's resolution "Hayya applies 3 months to its e-visas" was dropped as a conclusion.
- Conflict moved out: health insurance. Visit Qatar (Qatar's tourism site) only "recommends" insurance and says it can be bought on arrival [V0006]; an older Visit Qatar page (archived April 2023) said it was required only for stays over 30 days [N0015]; Doha Guides (Nov 2025) says it is mandatory only over 30 days [N0001]. Law 22/2021 Arts 8 and 10, MoPH and MOI say it is required [R0013, R0014, V0133, V0062], and the doc states that rule only. Not kept as a Need to know: the law is clear.
- Conflict moved out: extension route. Hayya says all extensions go through the Directorate of Passport at HIA [V0025]; MOI has an online Visit Visa Extension service [R0017, V0060, R0018]. The doc presents both as routes rather than as a conflict.
- Conflict moved out (kept as one Need to know under Don't work): changing a visit visa to a work visa in Qatar. MOI's older FAQ (undated, archived 2022) says no, leave and re-enter [V0098]; the 2019 regulations allow it at the authority's discretion [S0038]; the Hayya CEO (Peninsula, April 2023) said Hayya can't be converted [V0128].
- Source note moved out: the A1 stay length. The residence regulations (Art 30) allow tourist and business visit visas of 30 or 60 days, or multiple entry for 6 months or 1 year (max 90 days per trip) [R0007]; Hayya issues the A1 for 30 days, single entry [V0031], which is what the doc states for the A1. R0007 is kept under Business visit.
- Fact cut as duplicate: Doha Guides says booking through Discover Qatar depends on visa type and nationality [N0008]; Hayya's own statement [V0016] and Discover Qatar's list [N0011, N0013] cover it.
- Fact cut as duplicate: Gulf News (June 2026) quoting an MOI airport officer repeating the QR 200 a day overstay fine and advising visitors to check their status on Metrash [V0127]; MOI's own page [V0094] is cited.
- Fact cut as fluff: an MOI officer (Gulf Times, June 2026) said HIA has 76 e-gates for citizens, residents and visitors [H0039]; does not answer whether first-time Bangladeshi visitors may use them (kept as Need to know).
- Fact cut as fluff: in 2012 the Expatriates' Welfare secretary said going abroad on visit visas to stay and work could harm Bangladesh's labour markets [N0025].
- Research narration moved out: the Qatar Embassy in Dhaka's consular page could not be read (behind the Need to know on MOI visit visas via the embassy) [V0042].
- Research narration moved out: Doha Guides details on Hayya delays: in October 2023 a strict security check delayed applications about 3 weeks with no official announcement, readers reporting 10-16 days pending [N0006]. Kept in the doc only as "10 days to 3 weeks pending" [N0005, N0006].
- Research narration moved out: the MoPH list of approved insurers is behind a captcha [V0110] (kept as Need to know).
- Source dates moved out: the practice reports on extensions are reader comments of March 2025 (insurance auto-extension) [N0003] and November 2024 / March 2025 (online extension failing) [N0004]; the 2014 tourist-visa case is from the Daily Star (2014) [N0023, N0024]; the Nigerian Hayya "work visa" scam is from The Peninsula (March 2023) [N0026]; the free-visa op-ed is Prothom Alo (May 2026) [J0003, N0027]; the Tk 4.5 lakh case is Daily Star (2018) [N0028]; the Dhaka gang is Daily Star (2019) [N0029]; the Ministry's "no free visa" statement is a news report of 21 Oct 2025 (Dhaka Tribune, Financial Express), Ministry release not found [V0129, W0003].
- Moved to another section: Doha Guides says for family visit visas the host gets an SMS link to buy the visitor's health insurance after approval [N0050]. It was in old SOP 20 but belongs to the Family visit visa section (another writer); the lead should check it is kept there.
- Citation fix: the old doc said the fine for not reporting a housed visitor is "up to QR 20,000", citing V0123; V0123 (Law 21/2015 Art 6) gives only the 24-hour reporting duty, and no fact in facts.csv supports the QR 20,000 figure. The figure was dropped from the doc. Open question: find the penalty for Art 6 in Law 21/2015 Art 38 or elsewhere.
- Open question: the A1 Scams section has no complaint route of its own (the old text had none either). The lead may want a pointer to the agency complaint route in the company work visa (old SOP 10).
- Open question: cross-references used in this part: "Step 5 of the company work visa" (MOI Visa Inquiry, old SOP 4), "Step 1 of the company work visa" (job offer check, old SOP 3), "Step 2 of the company work visa" (police clearance, old SOP 6), "Changing visa type", "Having a child in Qatar". The lead should check these match the final headings.

### If you want to change employer

- L0224 (2020 news quoting a labour ministry official: an absconding report filed before the request stops the change) turned into a Need to know.
- Narration removed ("HRW says", "The TIP report says", "Migrant-Rights.org says"); facts merged [F0021, L0205, F0060, F0037].
- "Change before your RP is cancelled" removed (own advice). Vialto (June 2026) claim that a local transfer isn't allowed after RP cancellation [H0043] kept as a Need to know, without naming the firm.
- NOC explained here (first use in doc order now that chunk 2 no longer uses it).

### If your employer reports you absent ("absconding")

- Conflict (kept as one Need to know at step 1): L0217 is a 13 Dec 2016 government statement (before the 2020 reforms) that an employer can't file an absconding report after a worker's complaint. F0058 (TIP 2025): officials say absconding charges aren't considered until disputes are resolved, but workers must contest in person. F0034 (Migrant-Rights.org, Aug 2023): the promised MOL-MOI integration was not implemented. F0031 (MOL official, Jan 2024, lead): an MOL-MOI online link stops deportation before a complaint is settled. The heading "Complain first" was our own conclusion; replaced with the government's own urging.
- L0174 wording corrected: the Embassy FAQ says a 5-year ban "if needed" (প্রয়োজনে) when the fine is unpaid, not "up to 5 years".
- CEID: the Embassy FAQ (bdemb-doha-faq.md) places both the Search and Follow-up Department and the Public Prosecution's residency office "inside CEID"; K0003 is the only line equating CEID with the Search and Follow-up Department. Doc uses the fix-brief wording "the CEID (Search and Follow-up Department) building" [K0003].
- Repeats cut to a cross-reference: the offence and penalty (L0107, L0114, L0120, L0118) are stated in full in "What can get you jailed or deported"; the Embassy labour-wing phone and email (L0158) are in "If you aren't paid", step 8.
- Research narration removed: "Migrant-Rights.org says", "HRW says", "The Embassy warns" (facts kept with ids F0033, F0035, F0036, F0023, L0174).

### Leaving at the end of the job

- 90 vs 14 days: law (L0111, S0034) says leave within 90 days after the RP expires or is cancelled. Gulf Times 16 Jun 2026 (H0037) and Gulf News 17 Jun 2026 (H0006) quote Capt. Ali Ahmed Ali al-Kuwari (MOI Airport Passports Department) at an MOI webinar: 14 days after cancellation (earlier 30), then QR 10 a day. Vialto (H0043, 23 Jun 2026): staying past 14 days may lead to a blacklist. KPMG (H0008) repeats it. No MOI decision or Al Meezan amendment found. Kept as the single Need to know at step 5; elsewhere the doc should refer to this step.
- Deloitte (H0009, H0010, Feb 2025): MOL contract-termination approval before MOI cancels the RP; 5-7 working days. News-only procedure, now a Need to know at step 4.
- Supreme Judiciary Council (H0011, Peninsula Mar 2022, lead; original tweet not saved): travel-ban circulars in Metrash2, phones 55788372, 44597769, 44597777. Metrash2 replaced by Metrash on 1 Mar 2025 (M0049). Kept inside the travel-ban Need to know.
- Repeats cut to a cross-reference: gratuity amount and basis (L0013, L0014) and leave cash (L0036) are in "Your rights at work"; the guarantor rule (L0117) in "What can get you jailed or deported"; exit permit and Exit Grievances Committee (L0209, L0103) in "How long you can stay"; one-year work-permit ban (L0009, L0005) in "Bans on coming back"; baggage rules (X0052, X0053, X0056, X0059) in "Coming home, What you can bring home"; airport welfare desk complaints (B0079) in "Coming home". Ids L0014, L0005, X0053, X0056, B0079 no longer cited in this chunk.
- Research narration removed: "Deloitte (an advisory firm) says", "The MOI officer said", "Migrant-Rights.org reports".

### Tourist visa: Hayya A1
- Cut (repeat, no action value): Qatar's residence law (Law 21 of 2015, Art. 2) lets a foreigner enter only with a valid passport and an entry visa stating the purpose of entry [V0114].

Discover Qatar says booking with it is mandatory "for certain nationalities" on Hayya without naming them (N0011); its named list (India, Pakistan, Thailand, Iran) is for visa on arrival (N0013). The doc no longer reads the VOA list as the Hayya list. The gloss "Qatar Airways' destination management (tour) company" [Q0010] is left to the first use (L66) per the round-2 term decision.

- E-gates: Qatar Airways says people who have entered Qatar before can use the e-gates [V0109]; Visit Qatar's Hayya guide tells A1 travellers to use them [V0055]. Kept as one Need to know.

- Removed "MOI lists no documents for a newborn" (search narration). K0002's list is from MOI's first-time RP page for people who came on a residence visa; now inside the Step 4 Need to know.
- Doha Guides (expat guide, Nov 2025) conflicts with official figures: 60 days to apply, QR 10 a day penalty after [S0133]; QR 200 visa + QR 500 a year QID [S0135]. Official: 90 days (Law 21/2015 Art. 13) [S0035]; QR 400 (MOI) [E0066]. The 60 days in the regulations (Art. 49) is for babies of mothers on visit visas [R0025]. The doc keeps the official figures in the text and the guide's in the one Step 4 Need to know.

### Sending money home / What can get you jailed
- No saved fact gives a penalty for hundi or unlicensed currency exchange in Qatar (only MOI's appeal [H0027] and the January 2024 CID arrest [H0026]). The Sending money home pointer to "the penalties" was removed. For a later research round: the Qatari legal penalty for unlicensed money transfer or exchange.

### Student visa
- No saved source reports agents selling fake student visas; the doc now has a Need to know.

### Hayya A2
- "The 'others' option does not change the approved-professions rule" was our own reading of V0033 + R0009; now a Need to know.

### If a worker dies
- H0033's quote does not hold the "six families" statement; new fact GE0005 quotes it. The six were migrant workers generally, not only Bangladeshis.
- L0093/L0097 quotes do not cover heirs or death; new facts GE0002 (WSIF Decision 2/2022 Art. 5, last paragraph) and GE0003 (Art. 16, item 2).

### Facts re-quoted
- R0026 (Sahem) quote is about exchange students; the "contract expires upon graduation" line is now GE0004 (R0026 still cited at the Student visa lines for eligibility).
- QVC fee warning [A0043] is a Pakistan news item; the doc now says so.

### Round 4

- **Background, Where things stand (L33):** cut "Qatar is open:" (own conclusion; BMET figures only) [W0051, B0131].
- **Which law covers you (L293):** QatarEnergy glossed as "called Qatar Petroleum in the law's earlier text" (new fact GF0001, Law 14/2004 consolidated text, superseded wording). No saved source describes QatarEnergy as Qatar's state energy company, so that gloss was not used.
- **Health insurance and care (L317):** the 2024 QRCS interview claim (Mesaimeer and Al Hemaila 24/7 urgent care, free medicines, any ID accepted instead of a valid health card) is news-only; now a Need to know [F0050].
- **Fake government sites (L473):** removed "MOL's own e-services are only on addresses ending in mol.gov.qa" and "(MOL's in mol.gov.qa)": MOL's March 2026 statement [F0013] conflicts with MOL's own contract portal being on hukoomi.gov.qa [W0070]. Doc now says government sites end in gov.qa [N0030] and points to the official addresses in the steps. Note: L602 (Moawen section) still says all MOL e-services are only on addresses ending in mol.gov.qa [F0013-type claim]; not in the r4 list, left unchanged.
- **Hayya booking proof (L626):** "PDF under 2 MB, can re-upload if rejected" is from a Doha Guides page only [N0009]; now a Need to know. The Hayya UI strings transcript has no file-size rule.
- **A1 arrival (L668):** dropped "at immigration": the regulation [R0027] says fingerprints are taken before entry but names no place. The existing Need to know on e-gates covers where.
- **Student visa (L946):** removed the repeat of QU Student Employment Program / Sahem / HBKU hours [S0026, R0026, N0056]; these stay under "Can students work?".
- **Sending money home (L1114):** removed "Q-Cash" (no saved source explains the network) [B0125]; wording now "BRAC Bank and City Bank ATM booths, among others".

## Open questions (Need to know)

Every "Need to know" in the doc before the restructure, by section. The doc keeps the ones that change what a migrant does.

- **Introduction:** whether any nationality quota applies to Bangladeshis by company or sector. MOL's process for a recruitment approval sets gender, number and profession and then passes the request to a "nationalities selection system", without saying how nationalities are chosen.
- **Work visa: company worker:** any government-to-government (G2G) channel for Qatar; none was found.
- **Work visa: company worker:** whether the pilot still runs and which agencies take part, from an official source.
- **Work visa: company worker:** which five TTCs and how a worker applies; the Ministry's post does not say.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 1: Find a real job:** that list, and whether any accredited centre is in Bangladesh.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 1: Find a real job:** where a worker can check whether a sub-agent is registered; no public register was found.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 1: Find a real job:** whether BMET issues, but doesn't post, permission letters for Qatar company jobs.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 1: Find a real job:** whether that service is free and open to the public.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 1: Find a real job:** a Qatari rule that the employer itself may not recover visa or recruitment costs from the worker; the fee ban found applies to licensed recruiters.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 1: Find a real job:** the current maximum for Qatar. News reported Tk 100,780 for Oman and Qatar (fixed for 16 countries, reported in 2018 and 2025), and a district employment officer was quoted in 2025 as saying Tk 1.15 to 1.2 lakh for six Gulf countries including Qatar (unofficial, not verified).
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 1: Find a real job:** a current official figure in taka for what Qatar-bound workers pay. The latest official statistic found, from the Bangladesh Bureau of Statistics' 2020 survey, puts the recruitment cost for Qatar at 18.1% of a year's income earned there.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 2: Passport, police clearance and certificates:** how to correct a name spelling on a passport, and what QVC or MOI does when the visa name differs from the passport.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 2: Passport, police clearance and certificates:** the official fee notification and the processing time.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 2: Passport, police clearance and certificates:** whether workers with no certificates are asked for one; QVC says the appointment letter gives the full list.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 2: Passport, police clearance and certificates:** Bangladesh MOFA's fee and processing time for attestation (the MyGov service list did not load); the Qatar Embassy in Dhaka's own fee in Tk, hours and documents for legalising a police clearance or certificates, and how long a police clearance stays valid for Qatar.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 3: The employer gets your work visa:** how long MOL's recruitment approval, MOI's visa and the QVC result each take; the MOL, MOI and QVC pages give no time, and QVC says only that finishing the medical does not mean the visa is issued.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 4: Qatar Visa Center: contract, fingerprints and medical:** whether QVC Sylhet takes appointments in Nov 2026; call QVC on +880 9666 777 101.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 4: Qatar Visa Center: contract, fingerprints and medical:** whether any QVC fee may be charged to the worker.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 4: Qatar Visa Center: contract, fingerprints and medical:** an official statement that Qatar-bound workers need only the QVC medical.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 4: Qatar Visa Center: contract, fingerprints and medical:** the official list of conditions that make a worker unfit, any appeal, and how long a QVC result stays valid; forum users say 60 to 90 days (unofficial, not verified).
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 5: Check your visa is real:** which fields a genuine MOI Visa Inquiry result shows (for example the employer's name and the job), and the address of BMET's online visa check.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 5: Check your visa is real:** any appeal or re-application route if MOI rejects the visa; Visa Inquiry shows only the reasons, under Remarks.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance, insurance and the flight:** whether the premium changed with the welfare fee rise.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 6: Orientation, BMET clearance, insurance and the flight:** who pays the airfare for male company workers under Qatari law; the fee ban found covers licensed recruiters.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 7: After arrival:** who pays the QR 100 Medical Commission fee for company workers.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 7: After arrival:** whether the worker may be charged any of these fees.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 7: After arrival:** whether the employer or the worker pays the late fine.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 7: After arrival:** an official source on whether you keep a copy of the contract you sign at QVC.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 7: After arrival:** the CRA's own rules on how many lines one person may hold and whether a line registered on a passport must be moved to the QID (cra.gov.qa was not reached; the CRA's view above comes only from a news report, unofficial, not verified), and Ooredoo's document list for a new Hala line (its tariff does not list documents).
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 7: After arrival:** MOI's official registration steps.
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 7: After arrival:** the QCB circular itself (not found on qcb.gov.qa), and whether a worker can open a salary account before getting the QID (unofficial, not verified).
- **Work visa: company worker > Step 7: After arrival:** whether the residents' phase of the insurance law is enforced in practice.
- **Work visa: company worker > Your rights at work:** which law sets these workers' rights at work, and whether BMET clears Bangladeshi workers for these jobs.
- **Work visa: company worker > Your rights at work:** typical wages by trade for Bangladeshi workers in Qatar; the only official figures found are the minimum wage above and the one BMET permission letter for Qatar (women housemaids), which gives a salary of 1,000-1,200 with no currency stated.
- **Work visa: company worker > Your rights at work:** an official phone number for housing complaints; MOL's general grievance channels are listed under If you aren't paid.
- **Work visa: company worker > Your rights at work:** whether compulsory insurance for resident workers is enforced in practice; no Ministry of Public Health (MoPH) notice on it was found.
- **Work visa: company worker > What can get you jailed or deported:** an official MOI warning about carrying parcels for other people; the law already punishes transporting drugs even without intent to trade or use them.
- **Work visa: company worker > What can get you jailed or deported:** whether an ordinary unpaid debt (not a cheque) can lead to prison or a travel ban; only the cheque articles were found in the Penal Code.
- **Work visa: company worker > If you aren't paid:** which number MOL now gives for wage complaints, and the address of its online complaint platform; MOL's own website could not be read.
- **Work visa: company worker > If you aren't paid:** the Fund's platform address, office and processing time; its website could not be reached.
- **Work visa: company worker > If you want to change employer:** MOL's official change-employer guide and any fee; MOL's website could not be read.
- **Work visa: company worker > If your employer reports you absent ("absconding"):** how a worker checks whether a report has been filed (for example in Metrash, MOI's current app with more than 400 services) and the current official way to contest it; the only official steps found are the Embassy's 2021 FAQ.
- **Work visa: company worker > Leaving at the end of the job:** whether the Council of Ministers has approved this system, whether it covers private-sector migrant workers, and what it changes for the gratuity; the decision only creates the committee, and no later decision was found on Al Meezan.
- **Work visa: company worker > Leaving at the end of the job:** the official way to check and lift a travel ban (unofficial, not verified).
- **Work visa: company worker > Leaving at the end of the job:** where travellers get the Customs declaration form; the Customs authority's own traveller page was not found, and the English text of the Regulations on Al Meezan is a machine translation.
- **Work visa: company worker > Going home on leave:** whether a company worker must use it.
- **Work visa: company worker > Going home on leave:** where and how to apply for the return visa and its fee; a guide says return permits are among the travel services in the Metrash app (unofficial, not verified).
- **Work visa: company worker > Going home on leave:** where the sponsor applies, the fee and how long it takes.
- **Work visa: company worker > Going home on leave:** who pays the ticket for annual leave for company workers, since no Labour Law article on it was found. Domestic workers get a return air ticket home every two years.
- **Work visa: company worker > Scams:** where a worker can check the register of sub-agents.
- **Work visa: company worker > Scams:** the official MOL list of licensed offices, which could not be reached (unofficial, not verified). Protection: SOP 2, steps 9-11 (the Qatar-side checks); see Domestic worker.
- **Work visa: company worker > Scams:** an official Qatari or Bangladeshi warning about fake ages, fake certificates or borrowed identities at QVC.
- **Work visa: domestic worker:** whether workers on that boat, farm or estate visa are covered by the Domestic Workers Law or the Labour Law; the regulation does not say.
- **Work visa: domestic worker:** whether BMET clears Bangladeshi workers for boat, farm or estate jobs in Qatar; BMET's information page lists only one Qatar permission letter, for women housemaids.
- **Work visa: domestic worker:** whether BMET has issued any newer Qatar domestic-worker permission letters; its information page lists only the 2025 letter.
- **Work visa: domestic worker > Who pays for recruitment:** the Bangladesh-side cost rule for men going as domestic workers (house drivers, cooks, gardeners); the rule found covers women only.
- **Work visa: domestic worker > Moawen and licensed recruitment offices in Qatar:** Moawen's official steps, fees and whether workers can see their case on it; the MOL website could not be reached, so only news reports exist (unofficial, not verified).
- **Work visa: domestic worker > Moawen and licensed recruitment offices in Qatar:** the current MOL list itself; it could not be downloaded (reported only: unofficial, not verified).
- **Work visa: domestic worker > Moawen and licensed recruitment offices in Qatar:** whether the Group Visa fee is per worker, how long the committee approval and the visa take, and any appeal if the visa is refused; MOI's page (undated) gives none of these.
- **Work visa: domestic worker > Step 1: Check you qualify:** any Bangladesh-side age limit, test or licence rule for men going as house drivers; none was found, only Qatar's 18-60 limit.
- **Work visa: domestic worker > Step 2: Register on OEP and (women) take the housekeeping course:** the course fee in taka; the policy says only "the set fee".
- **Work visa: domestic worker > Step 3: Choose an approved agency:** the current approved list for women domestic workers; only the 1 Sep 2025 order was found.
- **Work visa: domestic worker > Step 6: Check the visa, then the medical and visa steps:** whether household (domestic) visas are processed at the Qatar Visa Center (QVC) in Bangladesh like company visas; no official page says so, and SOP 5 describes the company-visa route only.
- **Work visa: domestic worker > Ending the job and changing employer:** an official MOL page on how a domestic worker changes employer, including the notice period; only the general rule was found.
- **Work visa: domestic worker > If you aren't paid or are abused:** whether WEWB acts on such a request for a worker in Qatar.
- **Work visa: domestic worker > Bringing family:** who can sponsor the residence permit of a baby born in Qatar to a domestic worker who has no family residence; the newborn rule found is for residents licensed to have their family with them.
- **Tourist visa: Hayya A1:** whether the Qatar Embassy in Dhaka issues MOI visit visas to Bangladeshis or helps with Hayya; the embassy's consular page could not be read. Hayya points only to its own platform for the A1.
- **Tourist visa: Hayya A1 > Extending your stay:** the official A1 extension fee and the maximum total stay. Reported: many visitors extended 30 more days (unofficial, not verified).
- **Tourist visa: Hayya A1 > If you are refused:** any appeal route after an A1 refusal; Hayya and MOI state none.
- **Tourist visa: Hayya A1 > Step 4: Arrival at Hamad airport:** whether first-time Bangladeshi A1 visitors may use the e-gates.
- **Tourist visa: Hayya A1 > Step 4: Arrival at Hamad airport:** whether Qatar has any arrival card or e-registration; Hayya's list of what to carry does not mention one.
- **Family residence (spouse and children of a worker) > Who qualifies:** whether MOI changed the salary rule after December 2023; no newer MOI statement was found.
- **Family residence (spouse and children of a worker) > Attesting marriage and birth certificates:** the time and fee for MOFA attestation (not apostille) for Qatar.
- **Family residence (spouse and children of a worker) > Attesting marriage and birth certificates:** the steps and fee for attestation directly at the Qatar Embassy in Dhaka; through Qatar MOFA's service, the mission's fee is the equivalent of QR 120.
- **Family residence (spouse and children of a worker) > Fees:** the cost of the family members' health insurance; no official premium for residents was found.
- **Family residence (spouse and children of a worker) > If the visa is refused:** an official appeal route for a refused family residence visa; MOI's service page gives none.
- **Family residence (spouse and children of a worker) > Scams:** no source was found on family-visa scams (for example fake or bought marriage or birth certificates, or agents selling family visas); an issued visa can be looked up by its number on MOI Visa Inquiry (see SOP 4).
- **Family residence (spouse and children of a worker) > Can family members work?:** MOL's official page for this service, its fee and documents; mol.gov.qa could not be reached (reported only: unofficial, not verified).
- **Family visit visa > Step 5: Check the visa and travel:** how long MOI takes to decide a family visit visa, and whether a refusal can be appealed; MOI's page gives neither.
- **Family visit visa > Scams:** no source was found on family-visit-visa scams; an issued visa can be looked up by its number on MOI Visa Inquiry (see SOP 4).
- **Family visit visa > Personal visit visa:** whether MOI grants it to Bangladeshis; its page names no nationality.
- **Family visit visa > Personal visit visa:** any appeal if refused, and how long MOI takes to decide; MOI's page gives neither.
- **Business visit:** the current online route, fee and processing time for a Bangladeshi business, meeting or training visitor, whether a refusal can be appealed, whether the visitor can bring a spouse, and any reported scams; MOI's old service pages give an offline route through MOI Services Centres and a fee of QR 200, but no online route, processing time or appeal route.
- **Temporary work visa and work visit:** whether Bangladeshis are recruited on temporary work visas and whether BMET (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) clears them; the MOI visa fee, processing time and any appeal route; whether the worker can bring a spouse; and any scams reported for this visa; MOL's page names no nationality and gives none of these.
- **Temporary work visa and work visit:** whether Bangladeshis are issued the Multiple Entry Work Visit Visa and whether BMET clears it, its processing time, any appeal if refused, whether the holder can bring a spouse, and any scams reported for it; MOI's page and Article 48 give none of these.
- **Temporary work visa and work visit > Seafarers and truck drivers:** whether these visas are issued to Bangladeshis, their fees, where they are applied for, any appeal if refused and any scams reported for them; the regulations name no nationality and give none of these.
- **Transit:** whether a refused transit visa can be appealed (the Qatar Airways and Discover Qatar terms cover only refunds), any scams reported for transit visas, and whether the no-work rule Hayya states for its e-visas also covers a transit visa.
- **Student visa > Which universities are licensed (MOEHE list):** MOEHE's complaint route for an unlicensed institution or programme was not found; what is known is the list itself.
- **Student visa > Step 2: Apply for admission to Qatar University online:** other universities' visa steps were not researched.
- **Student visa > Step 2: Apply for admission to Qatar University online:** the amount of QU's admission application fee and QU's tuition fees for self-funded international students; the QU pages saved say only that the application fee is not refunded.
- **Student visa > Step 5: Wait for the committee's decision:** how long the committee takes, whether a refusal can be appealed, the MOI fee for a student RP and how long the student RP is issued for; none of these is on the QU pages saved.
- **Student visa > Scholarships:** scholarships at the University of Doha for Science and Technology and the other institutions on MOEHE's list were not researched.
- **Student visa > Can students work?:** MOL's own rule for off-campus part-time work by a student on university sponsorship outside Sahem (hours, permit, fee); no official page was found.
- **Hayya A2 and A3 (if you live in a Gulf country or hold certain visas):** any appeal route and any scams reported for the A2, A3 or A4; Hayya's FAQ gives neither.
- **Events, conference and medical visits:** where a Bangladeshi applies for the medical treatment visa, its fee, length and processing time; Hayya lists no medical visa, and its A1 covers tourism and family visits. A news site, Marhaba, reported in April 2026, citing Al Sharq, that the Ministry of Public Health is still exploring a medical visa with an electronic portal where patients abroad would submit their medical reports, with no launch date given (unofficial, not verified).
- **Events, conference and medical visits:** whether a refusal of any of these visas can be appealed, and any scams reported for them; Hayya's page and the regulations give neither.
- **Permanent residency, property and investor residence > Permanent residency (Law 10/2018):** the fee and card validity, which are set by an MOI decision that was not found. Also whether a refusal can be appealed and whether a permanent resident may work for any employer; the law has no article on either.
- **Permanent residency, property and investor residence > Property residence:** whether the list of areas changed after the 2023 manual.
- **Permanent residency, property and investor residence > Investor, entrepreneur, talent and researcher residence:** which state body handles each of these routes, the fees, processing time and whether Bangladeshis have received them; the regulations name no nationality.
- **Permanent residency, property and investor residence > Investor, entrepreneur, talent and researcher residence:** whether a refusal of property residence or of these residence visas can be appealed, and any scams reported for permanent residency, property residence or these visas; the Qatar Tourism property manual, the permanent residence law and the regulations give neither.
- **Changing visa type > The general rule:** how to apply for a change of purpose, the fee, and whether MOI grants it in practice; only the discretionary regulation was found.
- **Changing visa type > Visit visa to work:** a current MOI statement on whether any visit visa (Hayya, family visit, business) can be changed to a work RP inside Qatar.
- **Changing visa type > Family residence to work (join the labour market):** the official page of MOL (Qatar's Ministry of Labour) for its "Join the Labour Market" service (fee, documents), which could not be reached. Reported in news (unofficial, not verified):
- **Changing visa type > Domestic worker to company job:** whether a domestic worker can move into a company job covered by the Labour Law, and how; the law and the regulations allow a change of employer but do not describe a change of category.
- **Changing visa type > Change of profession on the RP:** the current MOL rules, documents and fee for changing profession; the MOL page could not be reached, and only the 2020 news report above was found (unofficial, not verified).
- **Asylum > UNHCR in Qatar:** whether any Bangladeshi has applied for or received asylum in Qatar, and how many people the committee has recognised; none was found, and the procedures were still being developed in December 2025.
- **Having a child in Qatar > Step 1: Get the Qatari birth certificate (MoPH):** the official MoPH online steps and fees for the birth certificate; the MoPH e-service could not be reached, and only the expat guide above describes it (unofficial, not verified).
- **Having a child in Qatar > Step 3: Get the baby a Bangladeshi e-passport:** which fee rate (worker/student or general) the Embassy charges for a baby, who has no Qatar ID yet.
- **Having a child in Qatar > Step 4: Apply for the baby's Qatar residence permit within 90 days:** MOI's official newborn RP procedure on Metrash, the fine for a late application, and the fee in practice.
- **Having a child in Qatar > If the parents have no family residence (women workers, students):** how a woman company worker or domestic worker without family residence, or a student, gets a Qatar residence permit for a baby born in Qatar, and whether the employer or university must agree; no MOI, MOL or university rule was found.
- **Sending money home > Where to send from in Qatar:** QCB's public list of licensed exchange houses, and which Qatar exchange houses have drawing arrangements with Bangladeshi banks (Bangladesh Bank's "Search Exchange House" tool could not be read).
- **Sending money home > The 2.5% incentive:** the current Bangladesh Bank circular on the incentive (its circular list sits behind a captcha); the budget speech is the official source found.
- **Sending money home > Saving in Bangladesh: Wage Earner Development Bond:** whether Arabian Exchange Co still sells the bond in Qatar; the Embassy page dates from 2021.
- **Sending money home > Hundi and unlicensed exchange:** how many Bangladeshis in Qatar use hundi and why; only news reports of an MOI warning (2022) and one arrest (2024) were found (unofficial, not verified).
- **Going home without valid papers > Travel Permit (outpass):** where the current Travel Permit form is available (Embassy counter or doha.mofa.gov.bd) (unofficial, not verified).
- **Going home without valid papers > Grace periods (amnesties):** any grace period in 2026; none was found on MOI pages or in the news (unofficial, not verified).
- **Going home without valid papers > Travel bans and re-entry bans:** the official MOI or court procedure to check and lift a travel ban; only news reports (2022, 2026) were found (unofficial, not verified).
- **If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job > Bangladesh side: who qualifies:** the QR amount the Embassy counter now charges for membership.
- **If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job > Bangladesh side: death benefits:** the official insurance claim form, document list and exclusions; news lists suicide or self-harm within 6 months, HIV/AIDS, war or riot, criminal attack and court execution (unofficial, not verified).
- **Coming home > Loans and support after return:** which RAISE services (cash incentives, training, counselling at district welfare centres) continue after June 2026.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 1: Register on OEP (Overseas Employment Platform) and take the training:** the registration fee; BMET's guide shows how to pay but not the amount.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 1: Register on OEP (Overseas Employment Platform) and take the training:** which 5 TTCs; the Ministry's post does not name them.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 2: Check a recruiting agency's licence (and its sub-agent/dalal):** where a worker can check the sub-agent register; the 2023 law says registration is "in the way set by rules".
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 2: Check a recruiting agency's licence (and its sub-agent/dalal):** the MOL list itself; the official copy could not be reached and the archived copy is damaged (reported via news, unofficial, not verified).
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 2: Check a recruiting agency's licence (and its sub-agent/dalal):** what "Server Locked" means for an agency; the list does not explain it.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 3: Check a job offer, demand letter and the Qatar employer:** whether a worker can check, with the Embassy, the reference number that BMET's 2025 permission letter prints beside the Qatar employer (for example "Ref. No: LW-QA-456"); the letter does not say what the number refers to, and the Embassy's attestation pages are from 2021.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 3: Check a job offer, demand letter and the Qatar employer:** whether BMET issues unpublished permission letters for Qatar company jobs; only one Qatar letter is posted.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 3: Check a job offer, demand letter and the Qatar employer:** whether the Company Inquiry Service is free and open without a Qatari login; the page could not be reached (unofficial, not verified).
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 4: Check a Qatar visa is real (MOI visa inquiry, Metrash):** the web address or app name BMET uses for the online check; BMET's charter does not give it.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 4: Check a Qatar visa is real (MOI visa inquiry, Metrash):** MOI's own description of the result fields and statuses; no official page shows a sample result.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 5: Book the Qatar Visa Center (QVC) and track your application:** what the booking form asks for and who normally books; the booking pages could not be read (unofficial, not verified).
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 5: Book the Qatar Visa Center (QVC) and track your application:** the official list of unfit conditions and any appeal; QVC says only that it may call you back or refer you for more tests.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 6: Get police clearance:** the processing time and how long Qatar accepts a PCC; the portal states neither.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 7: Get certificates attested (Bangladesh MOFA and Qatar):** how the MOFA attestation (not the apostille) is applied for, its fee per document and how long it takes; the MyGov service list did not load, and MOFA's five-day, QR-code description is for its e-Apostille, which Qatar does not accept.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 7: Get certificates attested (Bangladesh MOFA and Qatar):** the Qatar Embassy Dhaka fee, hours and documents; its page could not be reached.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 7: Get certificates attested (Bangladesh MOFA and Qatar):** what a genuine MOFA attestation for Qatar looks like; the QR code MOFA describes is on its e-Apostille certificate.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 8: Get BMET clearance and the smart card (incl. pre-departure orientation):** the click path for PDO booking on OEP; BMET's guide shows only the training path.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 8: Get BMET clearance and the smart card (incl. pre-departure orientation):** whether the housekeeping course replaces the 3-day PDO; BMET's charter lists them separately.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 8: Get BMET clearance and the smart card (incl. pre-departure orientation):** whether the insurance premium changed with the October 2026 fee rise; the Ministry's notices list shows no newer circular.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 11: After arrival: Qatar ID, Metrash registration, check your residence permit:** who pays the QR 100 medical fee for company workers; MOI's page states the fee only.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 11: After arrival: Qatar ID, Metrash registration, check your residence permit:** MOI's official Metrash registration steps; only the guide describes them (unofficial, not verified).
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 11: After arrival: Qatar ID, Metrash registration, check your residence permit:** whether QU or the student books the medical and fingerprints after arrival, and the student RP fee; QU's saved pages do not say.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 11: After arrival: Qatar ID, Metrash registration, check your residence permit:** what the expiry-date inquiry result page shows; Hukoomi describes only the inputs.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 11: After arrival: Qatar ID, Metrash registration, check your residence permit:** that office's official name and address; the Embassy FAQ gives neither.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 13: Complain about unpaid wages or abuse in Qatar:** any fee for an MOL complaint; the law sets the steps and time limits but no fee. The Labour Law exempts workers' lawsuits under the law or the contract from court fees.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 13: Complain about unpaid wages or abuse in Qatar:** the Fund's platform address and processing time; its site could not be reached.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 14: Change employer:** MOL's official change-employer guide and any MOI fee; MOL's service pages could not be reached (unofficial, not verified).
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 15: Join or renew the Welfare Board from Qatar:** the QR amount the Embassy counter charges now; no newer Embassy notice was found.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 15: Join or renew the Welfare Board from Qatar:** whether membership taken from Qatar expires and how it is renewed; the WEWB and Embassy pages explain only how to join.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 16: Send money home through a licensed channel:** Qatar Central Bank's list of licensed exchange houses; it was not found on qcb.gov.qa.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 17: Get a travel permit (outpass) if your passport is lost or expired:** where the current official form is; the Embassy page links only the old domain.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 17: Get a travel permit (outpass) if your passport is lost or expired:** whether a Travel Permit is accepted instead of a passport at that surrender, and the procedure for people who overstayed. The Embassy FAQ says to surrender with a valid passport, and adds that an expired passport can be extended at the Embassy and a lost one needs a police GD (general diary) and a Travel Permit; it does not say whether the surrender section accepts the Travel Permit.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 17: Get a travel permit (outpass) if your passport is lost or expired:** where the sponsor applies for that return visa, its fee and how long it takes; the regulation does not say.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 18: Claim death, injury, disability or job-loss benefits:** the insurance claim form and document list; WEWB and JBC pages do not give them.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 19: Apply for a Hayya Tourist Visa (A1) yourself:** any appeal after an A1 refusal; the Hayya FAQ gives none.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 20: Buy visitor health insurance:** the current list of MoPH-approved insurers; the MoPH page is behind a captcha.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 21: Before you leave Qatar for good:** where and how to apply for that return permit; the law says only that it is obtained from the competent authority before leaving or within one year of leaving, on payment of the fee; a guide says it can be started in Metrash (unofficial, not verified).
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 22: Register a baby born in Qatar:** which Embassy passport fee applies to a baby (worker or general rate); the Embassy says the fee follows the profession on the Qatar ID.
- **Standard procedures (SOPs) > SOP 22: Register a baby born in Qatar:** MOI's official newborn RP steps, documents and late fine. MOI's service page gives the QR 400 fee and the place (MOI Services Centres and Expatriate Affairs) and, for a residence permit on personal sponsorship, the form signed by the sponsor, the original passport, a photo and a blood group certificate, but no newborn-specific steps or late fine; for the rest the expat guide is the only source (unofficial, not verified).
- **Qatar as a destination > Workers going to Qatar:** why the two BMET figures differ; neither source explains it.
- **Qatar as a destination > Bangladeshis in Qatar:** an official Qatari count by nationality, to compare Bangladeshis with other nationalities in Qatar; Qatar's labour force figures split only Qataris and non-Qataris (2,112,589 non-Qataris of 2,239,938 employed in 2024) (unofficial, not verified).
- **Qatar as a destination > Remittances:** annual figures from Qatar for FY2023-24 onwards; Bangladesh Bank's country table found ends in April 2024.
- **Qatar as a destination > Trades in demand and growth:** which five TTCs, and how a worker joins this intake; the Ministry's post does not name them.
- **Qatar as a destination > Agreements, restrictions and the ILO:** the MoU's text; no official copy was found (unofficial, not verified).
- **Qatar as a destination > Agreements, restrictions and the ILO:** whether any G2G (government-to-government) recruitment channel to Qatar exists.
- **Qatar as a destination > Agreements, restrictions and the ILO:** an official record of any past restriction by either government; only news reports were found (unofficial, not verified).
- **Contacts > Qatar in Bangladesh:** QVC Sylhet's street address; QVC's page lists the city only.
- **Contacts > Qatar in Bangladesh:** its address, phone and hours (its pages could not be read). For document legalisation, QVC has an attestation counter that handles Qatar embassy legalisation through service providers.
