2. **Sub-agent registration rules and public register** (Act 2023 s.14A) [A0025]. Searched bdlaws, probashi, BMET, OEP.
3. **BMET online visa check URL/app for Qatar** [B0014]. Searched bmet.gov.bd menus, OEP, web (Bangla/English).
4. **What a genuine MOI Visa Inquiry result shows** (fields, employer, job). Needs a real visa number.
5. **MOCI Company Inquiry Service**: inputs, fee, public access [A0044] (blocked).
6. **Typical processing time** demand letter to flight for Qatar (no official or reliable source).
7. **Current Qatar-specific cost survey** (BBS 2020 report PDF; SEDS 2023 PDF not found on bbs.gov.bd via search).
8. **Official QVC Bangladesh fraud notice** (only Pakistan press copy [A0043]); official Ministry "no free visa" text (blocked in parts W/B/V).
9. **Qatar MOL licensed recruitment-office list** (official PDF blocked; see Blocked about manual file).

### Conflicts (official vs practice, and news vs news)
- **Who pays vs what is paid:** official rule is no fee to the worker from Qatari licensed recruiters [W0019], employer bears costs to arrival in BMET's 2025 letter [W0043], and QVC says appointments are free [A0043, lead]; in practice Bangladeshi workers report Tk 3-4.2 lakh [A0015-A0017], BBS 2020 Tk 4.02 lakh [B0170], USD 3,000-4,000 [W0103]. Show the rule as the rule, practice as unofficial.
- **Maximum cost (news vs news):** Tk 100,780 [W0104, A0006] vs Tk 1.15-1.2 lakh [A0011]. No official order found; both "unofficial, not verified".
- **Sub-agents:** the law allows only registered sub-agents [A0025-A0027]; in practice most workers pay unregistered brokers, often relatives [A0001, A0045, A0007].
- **OEP status vs validity date:** cancelled licences can show future validity dates [A0031] (not a conflict between sources, but a trap for readers).

### Blocked
See `blocked-parts/A.md`: MOCI Company Inquiry Service page; BBS Cost of Migration Survey 2020 and SEDS 2023 reports; sub-agent registration rules; BMET online visa-check page; QVC Bangladesh fraud notice; the source of `manual/mol-licensed-offices.pdf`.


## D: D

## Findings, part D: practical pass, tests and documents (Bangladesh to Qatar, work visa)

Researched 7 Nov 2026. Ledger ids are in `ledger-parts/D.csv` (D0001-D0055; facts in `D.facts.csv`). Blocked pages are in `blocked-parts/D.md`.

**How to read this.** Official rows are `verified` or `possibly-outdated`. Practice rows from news, NGO and worker-forum sources are `unofficial` (what happens in practice) or `lead` (an exact fact that still needs an official source); in the doc both go under "Needs an official source" and practice facts carry "unofficial, not verified". The forum threads (expat.com) are mostly posts from Indian, Pakistani and Filipino applicants. QVC (Qatar Visa Center) runs the same process in all six countries, but **no Bangladesh-specific report of a QVC "unfit" case was found** in English or Bangla news (searched: Daily Star, Prothom Alo, TBS, Financial Express, Bangla terms "কাতার ভিসা সেন্টার", "মেডিকেল আনফিট", "কিউভিসি").

I did not duplicate part M (the user's QVC pages: M0001-M0043) or parts W/B. Where they already hold the official fact, I cite their ids.

Terms to explain inline: **QVC** Qatar Visa Center (MOI-mandated centre run by Biomet Services/SQ Services; biometrics, contract signing, medical). **MOPH** Qatar Ministry of Public Health; its **Medical Commission** decides medical fitness for residence. **WAFID** the Gulf Health Council's medical-check system for Gulf work visas (formerly called **GAMCA**). **MOFA** Ministry of Foreign Affairs (Bangladesh or Qatar; say which). **Attestation / legalisation**: stamping a document so another country accepts it; **apostille**: the single-stamp version used between countries in the Hague Convention (Qatar is not one).

---

### 1. Work visa, Step "QVC: contract, biometrics, medical" (practice)

**Who books and what it costs**
- Official, already in the ledger: booking uses the MOI reference after the employer's approval [W0089, lead]; the sponsor pays the lounge fee BDT 4,260 when booking online [M0022]; the biometric visa fee is collected by MOI, not by QVC's operator [M0024]; QR 490 contract service is an employer fee [W0066]; value-added services are optional and fees non-refundable [M0025, M0026].
- Practice: at the Dhaka opening (Dec 2018) Qatar's ambassador said QVC would give visa services and medicals "without any cost for all visitors" [D0026, unofficial]. QVC Kathmandu's head said no worker pays for the medical and QVC asks agencies to refund workers who were charged (Nepal) [D0028, unofficial]. No Bangladesh statement on refunds found.
- The medical at QVC Dhaka is run by a contractor, Stemz Healthcare (2018) [D0027, unofficial].
- In the ILO-backed pilot, 7 selected workers were still charged by local middlemen; the agency got the money back [D0032, unofficial]. Pilot partners named by the ILO Qatar office: QDVC, its service provider STS, and Bonanza Overseas [D0031, lead] (closes part W's "Need to know" on the partner names, still only a lead).

**Centres and capacity**
- QVC's own site lists Dhaka and Sylhet and says Sylhet is "accepting appointments" [M0013, M0014]. In May 2026 the Bangladesh minister said there is "only one visa and medical centre in Dhaka" [D0034, conflict; same as W0009]. See Conflicts.
- No 2025-2026 report on queues or appointment shortages at QVC Dhaka was found. The minister's request for centres in all 8 divisions cites "suffering, travel costs, and delays" (W0009 lead) but gives no figures.

**What the medical tests for (official)**
- QVC publishes only the steps: blood sample, vaccination, eyesight, X-ray, doctor's exam [M0008, M0009]; bring glasses, specialist reports and past TB records/X-rays [M0029, M0031].
- The closest official test list is Qatar's Medical Commission fitness form (MOPH, 2020): chest X-ray, creatinine (kidney), HIV, hepatitis B (HBsAg, Anti-HBs), hepatitis C (Anti-HCV, HCV PCR), syphilis (RPR), TB (PPD skin test, Quantiferon TB) [D0001]; a signed declaration on epilepsy, asthma, psychiatric disorders, hearing problems [D0002]; blood pressure, pulse, height, weight, vision; result FIT or UNFIT [D0003]; Anti-HBs marked "for HBV vaccination" [D0004]. This is the in-Qatar form; say so in the doc.
- **Need to know:** the official list of conditions that make an applicant "unfit" (searched moph.gov.qa via search engine, Hukoomi, Al Meezan by search; only news from 2010-2016 and forums).

**Common "unfit" reasons in practice (unofficial)**
- Hepatitis B: forum users say an active virus leads to "unfit"; bring a specialist report [D0045]. HIV, hepatitis B/C, sexually transmitted disease [D0041].
- TB: a past-TB scar on the chest X-ray usually means "unfit"; an abnormal X-ray plus a positive skin test is treated as past TB [D0044]; NGO: workers classed unfit for treated TB scars across the Gulf [D0052].
- Diabetes/high blood sugar: referral to hospital and re-test within 30 days [D0041].
- Eyesight, epilepsy: QVC asks for specialist referrals (ophthalmologist, neurologist); even with "fit" letters one applicant was declared unfit [D0036].

**Re-test, referral and results**
- Official: QVC can call the applicant back for a medical re-examination or refer him to local healthcare providers for more tests, on MOH requirements [M0038]; status "Medical Complete" means reports went to MOH for validation [M0037]; "Result Available" means the employer can see the visa result [M0036]; biometrics/medical done does NOT mean a visa [M0035].
- Practice: referral X-rays and CT scan plus physician review [D0038]; status "Referral medical report pending with authorities" for 10-25 days; forum advice: call QVC Qatar +974 44069999, and after 15 working days the sponsor raises a ticket [D0046]; "result sent to employer" usually means fit (forum) [D0042].
- The worker is not given the report; the employer says "cancelled and rejected, medically unfit" [D0035]; a complaint to QVC got "contact your sponsor", and the sponsor got only a one-line rejection [D0039]; forum: the sponsor never gets the MOPH report, only the status [D0040].
- After "unfit": the employer can file a new visa and pay again; QVC sometimes repeats tests [D0037]. Forum claim: medical results valid 60-90 days [D0043, exact fact, unofficial].
- **Appeal:** no official appeal route for a QVC/MOPH "unfit" result found. The Gulf Health Council's new WAFID rules (2026) add a Grievance Committee and a 3-month retest bar, but these apply to WAFID medicals, not QVC [D0051, unofficial]. **Need to know** (searched MOPH, Hukoomi, QVC pages saved by the user, news, forums).
- **Time to result:** no official figure. Forum: 10-25 days in "referral pending" [D0046]; part W/M have no official time either.

**Arrival medical (conflict)**
- Official: medical in Qatar within 30 days of entry, then fingerprints [W0078]; Medical Commission exam with the home-country certificate, QR 100 [W0076, W0077]. NGO (2026): QVC-tested workers do not usually undergo another medical on arrival [D0053, conflict]. See Conflicts.

### 2. Is a separate medical (GAMCA/WAFID) needed for Qatar or for BMET clearance?

- Official Qatar: the work visa needs "pass the medical test outside the country" [W0030]; in Bangladesh that is done at QVC [M0006-M0009].
- NGO (May 2026): people seeking jobs in Qatar are exempt from WAFID medical testing and do the medical at QVC [D0050, lead].
- Wafid's booking form still lists Qatar as a destination and Bangladesh as an exam country [D0025, official, form option only].
- BMET clearance documents (agency or own visa) do not include a medical certificate [B0050, B0052]; BMET's safe-migration checklist lists "medical certificate" among papers to carry [B0008]. That reading (no medical needed for clearance) is ours from the lists.
- Practice: in the ILO pilot workers "passed their medical exam" before the visa and QVC [D0032]; which medical that was is not stated. Commercial guides still tell Bangladeshis to do a GAMCA medical for Qatar (not ledgered; aggregator sites).
- **Need to know:** an official statement (BMET, Ministry, Qatar MOI/MOPH or GHC) that Qatar-bound workers need only the QVC medical, and whether agencies charge for a "pre-medical" before QVC.

### 3. Skills and trade tests

- Qatar law (Law 9/2026): some professions need a training-and-test certificate from an MOL-accredited centre; list on MOL's site [L0073]. List not found (mol.gov.qa blocked, part W/L).
- Food handlers (restaurants, catering): since Sept 2026 MOPH's food-handler licensing exam can be taken at accredited centres abroad; optional, not a recruitment requirement; passing avoids re-taking it in Qatar [D0029, D0030, lead]. **Need to know:** whether a centre is in Bangladesh (MOPH page blocked).
- Drivers: QVC vision tests count in Qatar [W0096, lead].
- Qatar asked (May 2026) for electricians, plumbers, AC technicians and welders from five TTCs [W0007, W0008, lead]. Which TTCs, and any trade test: **Need to know** (also in part W).
- BMET wants skill certificates before the smart card in the one Qatar permission letter seen [W0042].

### 4. Passport

- QVC books only passports with more than 195 days' validity (since 9 Sept 2023) [M0016]; QVC tells applicants to renew and inform the sponsor before booking [D0048].
- Renewing in Bangladesh (official, July 2026): 48 pages/5 years Tk 4,025 regular, Tk 6,325 express, Tk 8,625 super express; 10 years Tk 5,750/8,050/10,350 [D0019]; delivery 15 working days (regular), 7 (express), 2 (super express) after biometrics [D0020]; pay online (A-Challan, DGePay, ShurjoPay incl. bKash/Nagad) or at any bank [D0021].
- Name spelling: the e-passport is filled exactly as on the NID (or online birth certificate) [D0022]; NID is required over age 20 [D0023]; bring the original NID [D0024]. The OEP registration also follows the NID [B0001]. So the NID is the master spelling.
- In practice, name mismatches between passport, appointment letter and medical papers happen; forum advice: report it at QVC reception [D0047]. QVC's name rule (three names in passport order) is posted for India only [D0049].
- **Need to know:** how to correct a passport name spelling (DIP correction rules; epassport.gov.bd items 11-15 not in the saved excerpt; only news found: spelling corrections allowed, name changes restricted); what QVC/MOI do when the visa name differs from the passport (no official source).

### 5. Police clearance and certificate attestation

- Qatar MOI: the work visa needs a home-country police clearance attested by the Qatar Embassy, and attested education certificates [W0029, W0028]. QVC's mandatory list: appointment letter, passport (195 days), academic qualification certified "by the concerned authorities in Qatar and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs of Qatar, finalized by the ratification of the local Embassy" with the original [M0017, M0018]. QVC's list does not mention a police clearance; it says the appointment letter has the full list [M0019].
- Bangladesh side (MOFA, official): SSC/HSC: Education Board, then Ministry of Education, then MOFA; degrees: university, Ministry of Education, MOFA [D0010]; passport copy, character/experience certificates, NID: notary, Law Ministry, MOFA [D0011]; medical certificates: Civil Surgeon + notary, Law Ministry, MOFA [D0012]; driving licence: notary + BRTA, Law Ministry, MOFA [D0013]; Arabic translations: notary + Islamic Foundation [D0014]; all documents need MOFA attestation [D0015]. Embassy Doha FAQ: a Bangladesh PCC is attested by MOFA [B0148]. MOFA's online system runs on MyGov, QR-verified, "within five days" [D0016, D0054]; contact Segunbagicha, +8802-223381852, dgcnw@mofa.gov.bd [D0017].
- Qatar is not among the 114 countries that accept Bangladesh apostilles [D0018; B0128].
- Qatar side (official): MOFA Qatar's "Complete Attestation Outside Qatar" sends foreign documents to the Qatari mission (here, the Qatar Embassy Dhaka) for attestation [D0005]; fees QR 150 + the equivalent of QR 120 for the mission + Bangladesh MOFA's fee [D0006]; apply online (e-services account) or at the Consular Affairs building, Sun-Thu 7:30-13:00; help 109 or 40111188 [D0007]; degrees only from universities on Qatar's accredited list [D0008]; online since Dec 2024 [D0009]. This route is applied for from Qatar (normally by the employer); it matches QVC's wording "certified by ... Qatar MOFA, finalized by the ... local Embassy" [M0017].
- QVC Dhaka has an attestation counter run through service providers [W0059].
- **Need to know:** the Qatar Embassy Dhaka's own fee in Tk, hours and documents for legalising a PCC/certificates (embassy site blocked for parts B, E, V and D); whether unskilled workers without certificates are asked for "academic qualifications" at QVC; PCC validity accepted by Qatar; Bangladesh MOFA attestation fee per document.

### 6. Documents that go wrong in practice, and the official fix

| Problem | What happens (unofficial) | Official fix |
|---|---|---|
| Passport under 195 days | QVC will not book [M0016] | Renew first (Tk 4,025+, 2-15 working days) [D0019, D0020]; tell the sponsor [D0048] |
| Name spelling differs (NID / passport / visa / appointment letter) | Mismatch at QVC; forum: report at reception [D0047] | Passport follows NID [D0022]; correction rules: Need to know |
| Certificates not attested | Visa requirement [W0028]; QVC mandatory [M0017] | Bangladesh chain to MOFA [D0010-D0015], then Qatar mission/MOFA [D0005-D0007] |
| Police clearance missing/unattested | Visa requirement [W0029] | PCC online (part B SOP 3 [B0043-B0046]), MOFA [B0148], Qatar Embassy [W0029] |
| "Unfit" result | No report, visa cancelled [D0035, D0039] | QVC re-examination/referral [M0038]; ask employer for status [M0036]; no appeal found |
| Missed appointment | Status "Appointment No show" [M0039] | Book again, or walk-in [M0039, M0028] |
| Biometrics failed | Status "Re-Enrolment Required" [M0040] | Return to QVC, contact QVC at once [M0040] |
| "Fit" certificate bought from a broker | WAFID/GAMCA context: workers then fail the arrival test [D0055, unofficial] | For Qatar the medical is done at QVC itself [M0008] |

---

### Draft SOPs

**SOP D1: Track your QVC application yourself**
- When to use: after the QVC visit, while you wait for the visa.
- Steps: 1) Open qatarvisacenter.com, choose "Bangladesh - English", then "Track Application" [M0034]. 2) Type your passport number and your visa number exactly as on the visa copy the employer/agency gave you, and the captcha; press Submit [M0034]. 3) Read the status (below). Free (no fee mentioned anywhere).
- What you see: Appointment Confirmed; Employment Contract Status; Biometric Enrolment Complete; Medical Test Complete; Medical Test Status; Results Available with Employer [M0042]. "Medical Complete" = reports sent to MOH to check; you may be called back for a re-test or referral [M0037, M0038]. "Result Available" = MOI processed everything; the visa decision is with the employer; ask the employer [M0036]. "Appointment No show" = book again [M0039]. "Re-Enrolment Required" = go back and redo biometrics; contact QVC at once [M0040]. In practice "Referral medical report pending with authorities" can last 10-25 days [D0046, unofficial].
- Remember: finishing biometrics or the medical does NOT mean a visa [M0035].
- If it goes wrong: QVC Bangladesh +880 9666 777 101 (Sun-Thu 08:30-17:00), info.bgd@qatarvisacenter.com; QVC Qatar +974 44069999 [M0003, M0033]; forum practice: after 15 working days unchanged, the sponsor raises a ticket [D0046, unofficial].

**SOP D2: Check your Qatar visa on MOI (Visa Inquiry)**
- When to use: before paying, and after QVC to see if the visa was issued.
- Steps: MOI Visa Inquiry (portal.moi.gov.qa > Inquiries > Visa Services; form at fees2.moi.gov.qa/moipay/visa/inquiry?language=en) [W0062, V0059]; choose "visa number" or "passport number"; type it; choose nationality Bangladesh; type the image code; press Inquire [V0059, W0063]. The "application number" search also needs the sponsor's QID and the application date [V0059].
- What you see: latest status; if rejected, the Remarks icon gives the reason; issued visas can be printed [W0063]. Check the employer and job match your contract (part W SOP W3).
- If it goes wrong: do not pay; BMET online visa check (Qatar is covered; free, 3 working days) [B0014, B0015].

**SOP D3: If you are told you are medically "unfit"**
- When to use: the employer or agency says the visa was cancelled for medical reasons.
- Steps: 1) Check the QVC status yourself (SOP D1). 2) Ask the employer, through the agency, to ask QVC/MOI which test failed (in practice workers are told only "unfit") [D0035, D0039, D0040, unofficial]. 3) If QVC asked you to come back or see a specialist, go: QVC can re-examine or refer you [M0038]; bring specialist reports and past TB X-rays [M0029, M0031]. 4) Treatable problems (e.g. high blood sugar) were re-tested within 30 days in reported cases [D0041, unofficial]. 5) If not treatable (e.g. reported: past TB scar, active hepatitis B), forum experience is that re-applying fails again [D0044, D0045, unofficial].
- Money: you should not have paid for the visa or medical (Qatar law bans recruiters charging workers [W0019]; QVC fees are sponsor/employer fees [M0022, M0024, W0066]). If an agency took money, complain (part B SOP 6: BMET or the Ministry) [B0016, B0020].
- **Need to know:** official list of unfit conditions, appeal route, how long a result is valid.

**SOP D4: Get your certificates and police clearance accepted in Qatar**
- When to use: the employer asks for attested education certificates or a police clearance.
- Steps: 1) Police clearance online (part B SOP 3) [B0043-B0046]. 2) Bangladesh attestation: SSC/HSC via the Education Board, then the Ministry of Education; degrees via the university, then the Ministry of Education; driving licence via notary + BRTA, then the Law Ministry; then MOFA for every document [D0010-D0015]; online via MyGov ("Certificate attestation for foreign-bound citizens") [D0016, D0054]. 3) Qatar side: the Qatar Embassy in Dhaka legalises it; MOFA Qatar's "Complete Attestation Outside Qatar" service (QR 150 + the equivalent of QR 120 for the embassy + Bangladesh MOFA fee) handles this from Qatar [D0005-D0007]. QVC Dhaka also has an attestation counter [W0059].
- What you get: documents with MOFA Bangladesh's stamp/QR, then the Qatar Embassy's stamp; Qatar MOFA's final attestation if done through Qatar [D0005].
- If it goes wrong: MOFA Bangladesh dgcnw@mofa.gov.bd, +8802-223381852 [D0017]; MOFA Qatar Attestation Department 40111188 / 109 [D0007].
- **Need to know:** Qatar Embassy Dhaka fee and hours.

**SOP D5: Renew a passport before QVC (195-day rule)**
- When to use: the passport has 195 days or less left [M0016].
- Steps: apply online at epassport.gov.bd, filling in names exactly as on your NID [D0022, D0023]; pay (Tk 4,025 regular / 6,325 express / 8,625 super express for 48 pages, 5 years) online or at a bank [D0019, D0021]; give biometrics at the passport office with the original NID [D0024]; wait 15/7/2 working days [D0020]; tell your sponsor/agency the new passport number before they book QVC [D0048].

### Need to know (with what was searched)

1. Official list of medical conditions that make a Qatar work applicant "unfit", and any re-examination/appeal route (MOPH via search, Hukoomi, Al Meezan search, QVC user-saved pages; QVC FAQ page blocked).
2. How long a QVC medical result stays valid; how long MOH validation takes (QVC pages, forums only).
3. Official confirmation that Qatar-bound Bangladeshis need only the QVC medical, not also a WAFID/GAMCA medical; whether agencies charge "pre-medicals" (Wafid site, BMET charter/leaflet, news; only the NGO lead D0050).
4. Whether QVC workers repeat the Medical Commission exam in Qatar (W0078/W0076 vs D0053).
5. Whether QVC Sylhet operates in Nov 2026 (QVC site says yes, minister May 2026 says one centre) [M0014 vs D0034].
6. Qatar Embassy Dhaka attestation fee (Tk), hours, documents (embassy site blocked).
7. Bangladesh MOFA attestation fee per document (MyGov list loads by JavaScript).
8. Police clearance validity Qatar accepts (MOI, QVC; nothing found).
9. Passport name-correction rules (DIP; only news) and the fix when visa and passport names differ (nothing official).
10. Professions needing Qatar's MOL training/test certificate (Law 9/2026) and whether any MOPH food-handler exam centre is in Bangladesh.
11. Which five TTCs feed Qatar's skilled-trade intake (part W too).
12. Whether QVC "Academic Qualifications" (mandatory) applies to workers with no certificates (QVC page says "refer to your appointment letter").

### Conflicts

- **Sylhet QVC:** M0013/M0014 (QVC official: Sylhet listed, accepting appointments; undated) vs D0034 (Prothom Alo, May 2026) and W0009 (Bangladesh Monitor): "only one visa and medical centre, in Dhaka". Official page is the rule; show both.
- **Arrival medical:** W0078 / W0076 (official: medical in Qatar within 30 days; Medical Commission exam QR 100) vs D0053 (NGO 2026: QVC-tested workers usually not re-examined on arrival). Official is the rule.
- **WAFID and Qatar:** D0025 (Wafid form lists Qatar as a destination) vs D0050 (NGO: Qatar job-seekers exempt from WAFID). Not a direct conflict; flag the gap.
- **Cost of QVC to workers:** D0026 (2018 ambassador: "without any cost") vs M0022 (lounge fee BDT 4,260, paid by sponsor) and M0025 (optional services paid at QVC by the applicant). Consistent if the worker pays only optional services; show both.

See `blocked-parts/D.md`: QVC FAQ page (the user's saved "faq" file is the Updates page), QVC booking form, MOPH Medical Commission fitness criteria, MOPH food-handler overseas centres, Qatar Embassy Dhaka attestation page (shared with B/E/V), MyGov MOFA attestation service list.


## F: F

## Findings, part F: practical pass, life and problems in Qatar (what actually happens, and the official fix)

Researcher F, 7 November 2026. Ledger: `ledger-parts/F.csv` (F0001-F0072). Blocked: `blocked-parts/F.md`.

This pass adds practice evidence to part L (the law and official procedures, L0001-L0228). Rules still come from L's official rows; F rows are mostly `unofficial` (what happens) or `lead` (a fact still needing an official source). Official rows in F: QNA (Qatar's state news agency reporting ministry statements: F0001-F0002, F0048-F0049) and the National Human Rights Committee's own site (F0007-F0009). BSS, Peninsula, Gulf Times, TBS, HRW, Amnesty, Migrant-Rights.org, ILO's annual report and the US TIP report are registered as non-official. The four `manual/mol-*.pdf` files were used only as leads and are not cited.

---

### 1. If you aren't paid (wages, WPS, complaints, committees, WSIF)

**How long it takes in practice (unofficial):**
- About 73% of MOL complaints are settled amicably within a week; the rest go to the Labour Dispute Resolution Committees. The law says the committee decides in 3 weeks (L0058), but cases often take several months up to a year [F0055].
- Complaints to the online platform: 31,549 (2022), 28,808 (2023), 22,158 (Jan-Sep 2024); about a quarter go to the committees, "a long period for workers to wait" [F0042]. Q1 2026: 5,369 complaints (L0201).
- Unpaid wages and benefits are the main complaint. Workers who find a new job can earn while waiting; others struggle or leave Qatar without their money [F0043; also L0221-L0223].
- Even with a committee verdict, money often does not arrive because no time limit exists for enforcement; all court documents are in Arabic [F0056]. Neither the committees nor the Fund cover the worker's transport and document costs [F0057].
- Many delays come from subcontracting: labour-supply companies tell workers they will be paid when the client pays; clients delayed up to a year [F0027, F0028]. HRW: WPS and the Fund are "insufficient" [F0029, F0021-F0026 context].
- WPS in practice: blocks a company automatically for non-payment or pay below the minimum wage, but cannot see pay below the contract wage [F0044, F0062]. MOL blocked 18,802 companies and issued 5,667 violations in Jan-Sep 2024 [F0062].
- Timing advice from a Ministry of Labour official (Jan 2024, via the embassy): complain or inform the embassy **within one month** of a missed salary; after 3-4 months it becomes hard to recover; after 2-3 months unpaid, look for other work [F0030, lead]. This matches the 2026 law's right to change employer when wages are not paid (L0067).
- Deportation while a case is pending: MOL said (2024) an MOL-MOI online link stops deportation before a labour complaint is settled [F0031, lead]; but Bangladeshi workers had been sent home with cases pending [F0032], and the US TIP 2025 report says authorities sometimes deported workers before cases were resolved [F0059].
- Protest or strike: in 2022 workers (including Bangladeshis) who protested unpaid wages were arrested and deported [F0072]; HRW 2026 says workers still strike despite the risk of arrest and deportation [F0024]. Lawful strikes have strict conditions (L0071).

**WSIF in practice:** funded by employers at QR 120 per worker per year; concerns about unclear eligibility, opaque decisions, fear of retaliation [F0057]. The Fund says it has "digital and in-person platforms" and runs workshops on how to complain [F0066, lead]. Official site unreachable (blocked).

### 2. Complaint channels: the hotline numbers (sources disagree)

No official MOL page could be read (mol.gov.qa: curl 000; Wayback copies are JavaScript shells; Firecrawl tunnel error/timeout). What the sources say:

| Channel | What is reported | Source, date | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| SMS 92727 | "5" + QID or visa number, 24 h, several languages | Gulf Times 2020 [F0014]; QL 2026 (L0194) | lead |
| 16008 | MOL line for workers' grievances | Gulf Times 2020 [F0015]; Qatar Living Aug 2026 (L0195); Doha Guides 2025 [F0018] | lead |
| 16505 | MOL hotline to report suspicious links/calls (fraud) | Peninsula Mar 2026 [F0012] | lead |
| 40488264 | summer work-ban violations, 2026 | Peninsula Jun 2026 [F0010] | lead |
| 40488248 | summer work-ban violations, 2025 | Peninsula Jun 2025 [F0003] | lead |
| 16044 | human trafficking reports | Peninsula 2022 [F0071] | lead |
| Online platform | QID/visa no. + mobile, or NAS; public reports by mobile only, sender not told outcome | Gulf Times/QNA 2021 [F0016, F0017] | lead |
| Status check | mol.gov.qa ServiceDetails itemid=47: establishment no. + complaint no. + year | Doha Guides [F0019]; L0199 | lead |
| Amerni app | reported "not working" since a 2023 update; no current store listing found | Doha Guides [F0018] | lead |
| NHRC | hotline 8002222; phone 44048844 / 66626663 / 33296666; fax 44444013; c@nhrc.org.qa; in person with printed form; online (login) | NHRC site, Nov 2026 [F0007-F0009] | **verified** |

Reading: 16505 is the number in the most recent MOL statement (2026), but only for reporting fraud; 16008 is the older complaints line still repeated in 2026 guides. Neither is confirmed by an MOL page. **Need to know:** which number MOL now gives for wage complaints (the manual lead file says 16505 and +974 44068979; not citable).

### 3. Changing employer, absconding, passport

- HRW 2026: in practice workers still need a signed resignation-approval letter from the old employer [F0021]; US TIP 2025: MOL officials reportedly still asked for an NOC, and employers retaliated by cancelling the visa or filing absconding during the notice period [F0060].
- Migrant-Rights 2023: absconding (huroob) reports used to block job changes [F0033]; employers used MOL's "Leave Country Notice" to cancel QIDs, which the worker could only lift in person at a labour office [F0035]; employers avoided gratuity by alleging absconding [F0036]; some workers forced to exit and re-enter [F0037].
- Contesting absconding: still in person at MOI's Search and Follow-up Department while employers file online [F0034 (2023), F0058 (2025)]. Officials say absconding charges are not considered until a labour dispute is resolved [F0058]. Practical rule from these: file the MOL complaint first (L0217).
- Passport: HRW 2026 says confiscation remains common and largely unpunished [F0022]; US TIP 2025: most workers' passports reportedly held by employers; 154 confiscation cases prosecuted only as residency/labour violations [F0061]. Official route stays L0098/L0099/L0144.

### 4. Heat (summer midday ban)

- Rule (official, L0078-L0082): 1 June-15 September, no outdoor or poorly ventilated shaded work 10:00-15:30; stop above WBGT 32.1. Confirmed applied again in 2026, schedule must be displayed [F0052, F0011]; also covers shaded/enclosed places without ventilation [F0005]; delivery by motorbike/bicycle banned in those hours (2025) [F0004].
- Enforcement: 388 sites closed for 3 days in 2026 (official, QNA) [F0001, F0002]; 368 in 2024 [F0046]. Report breaches: 40488264 (2026) [F0010].
- Practice: HRW says the 32.1 threshold is too high [F0026]; a Bangladeshi worker who died in Sept 2026 had told his wife they worked at night because of daytime heat [F0041]. Awareness "ambassadors" in workers' languages (MOL with WSIF) [F0006].

### 5. Accommodation

- Inspections: 5,063 accommodation sites inspected up to Sept 2024 [F0047]; 2,322 accommodation visits in Q1 2026 [F0054]; 5,035 companies found failing to provide suitable living conditions in 2024 [F0065]. Rules: L0083-L0087. **Need to know:** official accommodation complaint number (guides give 40280660; not fetched/verified).

### 6. Health care in practice

- Four QRCS Workers' Health Centers with MOPH (Al Hemaila, Mesaimeer, Fereej Abdul Aziz, Zekreet), 20+ specialties, some 24/7; 1.46 million visits in 2025 [F0048, F0049] (official via QNA).
- Mesaimeer and Al Hemaila give 24/7 urgent care; medicines free; **no valid health card needed, any ID accepted** (QRCS CMO, 2024) [F0050, lead]; emergencies call 999 [F0051]. Health card rules: L0149-L0153.

### 7. Deaths and repatriation of bodies

- HRW 2026 / Amnesty 2025: most deaths recorded as "natural causes", not investigated or compensated [F0025, F0069].
- Practice example June 2026: five Bangladeshis killed 21 June, bodies home 30 June; embassy Labour Welfare Wing did the legal steps, WEWB paid [F0038, F0039]; the ministry told the labour wing to secure compensation [F0040]. (Part H has the Tk 3 lakh payment, H0029.) Sept 2026 fall case: family unsure whether the body would come home [F0041].
- Official steps: L0163, L0179-L0182, L0015, L0050.

### 8. Bangladesh Embassy Doha labour wing in practice

- Embassy site has only thin labour pages (counselling, death, hospital visits, all 2021) and no notice on wages or shelters (notices and press releases checked 7 Nov 2026).
- Hotline conflict: embassy telephone page +974 33662000 (L0161, official) vs +974 33663000 in a Feb 2026 news report of an embassy statement [F0067, conflict].
- Practice: labour wing handled 2026 body repatriation (F0038); 2024 ambassador raised unpaid wages and deportation with MOL (F0030-F0032); during the 2026 Gulf crisis workers called embassy food aid insufficient (part H, H0013).
- Shelter: no Bangladesh embassy shelter in Qatar found. Qatar's government trafficking shelter (QRCS-run, up to 2 weeks, police notified on admission) also hosted workers awaiting labour cases [F0063]; Aman Center takes women who fled abusive employers, even if charges were filed [F0064]. **Need to know:** official Aman/NCCHT contact page.

### 9. Domestic workers

- Outside the Labour Law; abuse reported [F0070]; Aman Center [F0064]. (Domestic-worker route is another part's.)

### Draft SOPs (additions to L's SOP L3-L7)

**SOP F1 (merge into L3): Complain about unpaid wages, step by step.**
- When: salary not in your account by the 7th (L0088). Act within a month [F0030].
- Steps: (1) Keep payslips, bank statements, contract, QID copy (L0197). (2) Complain to MOL: online platform with QID or visa number + mobile, or NAS [F0016]; or SMS "5"+QID to 92727 [F0014]; or labour office in person (L0197). Phone: **Need to know** (16008 vs 16505, F0015/F0012). (3) The Labour Complaint Form (MOL, Wayback 2026) asks for name, QID, job, salary, questions on warnings/earlier complaints, and consent to referral to the committee; attach contract (Arabic) and ID copy (E0055, raw `mol-labour-complaint-form`, official MOL PDF via Wayback). (4) Note the complaint number; check status with establishment no. + complaint no. + year [F0019]. (5) Tell the embassy labour wing (L0158).
- What you see: status such as "Notice sent", "Sent to committee" [F0020].
- If it goes wrong: committee (L4); NHRC [F0007-F0009]; do not stop work or join a street protest (arrest/deportation risk [F0072, F0024]); ask to change employer (L0067).

**SOP F2: Report a summer heat-ban breach.**
- When: you are made to work outdoors 10:00-15:30 between 1 June and 15 Sept (L0078).
- Steps: call 40488264 (2026) [F0010] or the MOL email; the worker may stop and cannot be dismissed for it (L0081).
- What you see: inspection; site closed 3 days if confirmed [F0002].

**SOP F3: Complain to the NHRC (National Human Rights Committee).**
- When: serious abuse, passport held, long non-payment and MOL not acting.
- Steps: write the complaint clearly; give phone, email, address; attach passport or visa copy and documents; send by fax 44444013 or email c@nhrc.org.qa, or bring the printed form, or use the online form (login) [F0007, F0008]; hotline 8002222 [F0009].
- Cost: none stated. **Need to know:** response time.

**SOP F4: See a doctor without a health card.**
- Go to the QRCS Workers' Health Center urgent care at Mesaimeer or Al Hemaila (24/7), bring any ID; medicines free [F0050, lead]; emergencies 999 [F0051].

### Need to know (with what was searched)

1. Current official MOL complaint phone number (16008 vs 16505 vs +974 44068979): mol.gov.qa complaints page and ServiceDetails itemid=7/47 (curl 000, Wayback JS shells, Firecrawl tunnel error/timeout); Hukoomi; QNA; web search (only news/guides).
2. Whether the Amerni app still exists (App Store/Play searches found no current MOL listing; guide says not working).
3. WSIF application steps/platform URL/processing time (wsif.gov.qa: curl 000, no Wayback capture).
4. Official accommodation-complaint number (40280660 only in guides).
5. Embassy hotline: 33662000 (embassy page) vs 33663000 (news).
6. Aman Center / NCCHT shelter official contact; any Bangladesh embassy safe home in Qatar (none found).
7. Whether the MOL-MOI link really prevents absconding charges/deportation for workers with an open complaint (MOL 2024 says yes [F0031]; TIP 2025/MR 2023 say not in practice [F0058, F0059, F0034]).

### Conflicts

- **Embassy hotline:** +974 33662000 (official telephone page, L0161) vs +974 33663000 (Bangladesh Monitor, Feb 2026) [F0067]. Official is the rule.
- **Summer-ban hotline:** 40488248 (2025) [F0003] vs 40488264 (2026) [F0010]; both news, different years; use the 2026 one with "unofficial, not verified".
- **MOL hotline:** 16008 (2020; guides 2025-2026) [F0015, F0018, L0195] vs 16505 (MOL statement 2026, for fraud reports) [F0012]. No official source.
- **Absconding/deportation safeguards:** MOL official 2024 (online MOL-MOI link) [F0031] vs Migrant-Rights 2023 [F0034] and US TIP 2025 [F0058, F0059].
- **Committee time:** law 3 weeks (L0058) vs practice months to a year [F0055].

## H: H

## Findings, part H: practical pass, leaving Qatar and coming home

Researcher H, 7 November 2026. Ledger: `ledger-parts/H.csv` (H0001-H0053; facts in `H.facts.csv`). Blocked: `blocked-parts/H.md`. Builds on part L (Qatar-side law on leaving, travel permit, surrender) and part B (Bangladesh-side benefits, remittance costs); ids from those parts are cited where they are the official rule, and are not duplicated here.

Rows: 11 verified, 8 possibly-outdated, 3 conflict, 8 lead, 23 unofficial (53).

**Three new official sources found:** (1) National Savings Directorate's WEDB sheet, Aug 2026 (H0001-H0005); (2) Bangladesh Bank FEPD-1 Circular 41 of 8 Oct 2026 on the new Non-Resident Convertible Taka Account (H0018-H0023); (3) the Jiban Bima Corporation / WEWB migrant-insurance leaflet linked from the WEWB site (H0049-H0053), which settles several insurance details that part B had only from notices.

**Two practical warnings found:** (1) the Doha Embassy's own Travel Permit page links the application form to its old domain `bdembassydoha.org`, which now serves an Indonesian gambling site (H0042); (2) MOI officials now say a cancelled residence permit gives only 14 days to leave, not the 90 days in the law (H0037, conflict with L0111).

---

### 1. Leaving Qatar at the end of the job (feeds "Leaving at the end of the job" and SOP L8)

**The rule (part L):** dues by the next working day [L0020], gratuity at least 3 weeks' basic wage per year after 1 year [L0013], service certificate [L0012], ticket home at employer cost [L0016], RP cancellation signed by sponsor and worker [L0140]; exit needs no employer permission, Exit Grievances Committee in 3 working days [L0102, L0103].

**Gratuity in practice (unofficial):**
- Companies have sent workers home early, without full dues, sometimes calling it "long leave" so no end-of-service benefit is paid [H0035].
- Workers have signed (thumbprint) "receipt of all dues and discharge" papers they could not read, while still owed most of their gratuity; others were told on the day of the flight that dues would not be paid [H0036]. Link to L0096: a signed written settlement shelves a Workers' Support and Insurance Fund claim.
- Returnees report coming home with nothing after deportation for not having a valid work permit [H0014].
- Future change: PM Decision 34/2025 set up a Ministry of Finance committee on an investment-based end-of-service savings scheme that expatriates could opt into; no change yet to the gratuity formula [H0044, lead].

**RP cancellation and the 14-day window:**
- An MOI Airport Passports Department officer said at an MOI webinar (June 2026): leave within 14 days of the RP's cancellation (earlier 30 days); after that QR 10 a day [H0037; repeated by Gulf News H0006, KPMG H0008, Vialto H0043]. **Conflict:** Law 21/2015 (consolidated text on Al Meezan, read 7 Nov 2026) still says 90 days after expiry or cancellation [L0111]. No MOI decision, QNA release or amendment found.
- Vialto (advisory firm): the QR 10/day must be paid before leaving; overstaying the 14 days may lead to an entry blacklist; once the RP is cancelled the worker cannot transfer locally to a new employer and must be hired from abroad [H0043, unofficial]. Practical consequence for the doc: change employer (SOP L6) **before** the RP is cancelled.
- Deloitte (Feb 2025): employers must first get MOL contract-termination approval before cancelling an RP with MOI; 5-7 working days [H0009, H0010, unofficial].

**Before flying out (practice, MOI officer via Gulf Times):**
- Check Metrash for traffic fines, overstay fines and other dues and pay them in Metrash before going to the airport [H0038, H0007].
- If there is a travel ban, follow up with the department that issued it to have it lifted [H0038]. The Supreme Judiciary Council said in 2022 that travel-ban circulars can be viewed in Metrash2, or asked about on 55788372, 44597769, 44597777 [H0011, lead].
- 76 free e-gates at Hamad airport, 10-15 seconds [H0039].

**Fines (official, part L):** late RP steps QR 10/day up to QR 6,000; staying after the RP ends: settlement QR 12,000 or deportation [L0121, L0120, L0118].

### 2. Going home without valid papers (feeds "Going home without valid papers" and SOP L9/L10)

- **Travel Permit (outpass):** rule and fee in part L [L0154-L0157]; lost passport: police GD first [L0184].
- **The form:** the archived form (June 2024) is the Bangladesh High Commission London template reused by Doha; filled in block capitals, 3 copies with photos; asks name, parents/husband, date and place of birth, permanent address in Bangladesh (village/road, post office, upazila/thana, district), current address and phone, a reference person with address and phone, present passport number/date/place, height, eye/hair colour, identification marks, occupation, reason; declaration of no other Bangladesh passport [H0041].
- **Warning:** the form link on the official page (`bdembassydoha.org/pa/Travel_permit.pdf`) opened a gambling site on 7 Nov 2026 [H0042]. **Need to know:** where the current form is (counter or doha.mofa.gov.bd).
- **Grace periods:** last one 9 Feb-9 May 2025 [L0192, lead]; none found for 2026 (searched MOI, QNA, Gulf Times, Peninsula, web). In March 2026 MOI extended all entry visas by a month during the Gulf war (part V's row).
- **Crisis repatriation (practice):** in March 2026 the Embassy arranged a special Qatar Airways flight, priority for families, women, children, elderly, with sign-up through a Google Form on its official social media [H0040]; in April 2026 workers called embassy food aid insufficient [H0013], hotel staff faced 25% pay cuts [H0012].
- Airport desks give food and water to returnees coming back on an outpass or from jail [B0078].

### 3. Sending money home (feeds "Sending money home" and SOP 7)

**Official cost and channel (part B):** Bangladesh Bank cost data [B0119, B0120]; World Bank Q3 2025: QAR 730 cost 6.72-7.70% (QAR 20 fee + 3.98-4.96% exchange-rate margin), QAR 1,820 cost 5.08-6.06% [B0122, B0123]; bKash partners [B0124-B0126]; hundi warning [B0013].

**New official facts:**
- Bangladesh Bank allows banks to set up drawing arrangements with foreign exchange houses; money can go straight to the worker's own account or a relative's account in Bangladesh [H0016]; banks credit remittances within 3 days at most [H0017]. Bangladesh Bank's "Search Exchange House" tool (country = Qatar) lists Qatari exchange houses with arrangements, but the filtered result could not be fetched (POST form; firewall) [blocked].
- **NRCTA (Non-Resident Convertible Taka Account), FEPD-1 Circular 41, 8 Oct 2026:** savings/current/fixed account in taka at any scheduled bank's AD branch or OBU [H0018]; can be opened **before departure with no initial deposit** [H0019]; **remittances into an NRCTA generally do not get the incentive** [H0020]; interest at taka-deposit rates, freely repatriable [H0021]; operable by a relative under power of attorney, nominee paid on death, kept after return home [H0022]; loans against it, including for a home for own use [H0023].
- **Wage Earner Development Bond (National Savings Directorate sheet, Aug 2026):** Tk 25,000 to Tk 50 lakh denominations, 5 years; buy and cash at Bangladesh Bank, any AD bank branch, and abroad at Bangladeshi bank branches, exchange houses and exchange companies [H0001]; 12% at maturity up to Tk 15 lakh (11/10/9% for larger slabs), profit every six months [H0002]; lower if cashed early [H0003]; bought with remittance sent through legal channels; **no ceiling** [H0004]; tax-free, nominee, loan against it, 40-50% death-risk benefit, principal can be taken in US dollars [H0005].
- Bank guide (lead): the 2.5% incentive is only for wage earners' remittances; money used to buy WEDB/US dollar bonds gets no incentive [H0024, H0025].

**Hundi in practice:** MOI arrested a person of Asian descent for taking riyals and paying out an Asian currency (Jan 2024) [H0026, lead]; MOI urges use of approved exchange companies and banks only, warning against "attractive" rates outside banks [H0027, lead]. **Need to know:** any Qatar-specific evidence on how many Bangladeshis use hundi and why (searched English and Bangla news, NSU CMS, RMMRU; only Bangladesh-wide studies).

### 4. If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job (feeds that section and the WEWB SOP)

**Official, insurance (JBC/WEWB leaflet, 2024, contract of 12 March 2023 effective 1 Jan 2023):** Tk 10 lakh, 5 years, ages 18-55, Tk 1,000 once; **100% for natural death**, and for accidental death during cover or within 90 days after [H0049]; 100% total permanent disability; partial: 90/75/50/25/15% by injury [H0050]; Tk 50,000 if job lost and back within 6 months, **not if the employer paid the full migration cost** [H0051]; WEWB checks the papers and recommends to JBC, JBC pays WEWB; insurance certificate from the JBC website e-service [H0052]; JBC Group Insurance Division contacts [H0053].

**Official, Board benefits (part B):** Tk 35,000 at the airport [B0076, B0077], Tk 3 lakh grant [B0070-B0075], treatment aid up to Tk 2 lakh [B0081], compensation recovery through the labour wing with a power of attorney [B0086, B0146, B0151].

**Practice (unofficial):**
- A June 2026 Qatar road crash: bodies home in 9 days funded by WEWB; Tk 35,000 promised at Sylhet airport, Tk 3 lakh later [H0028]; Tk 3 lakh cheques handed over about 2 months after the deaths [H0029].
- Qatar compensation is rare because most Bangladeshi deaths are certified "natural causes" (71%, Nov 2016-Oct 2020) [H0032]; no post-mortem, so no compensation from employers or Qatar [H0033]; families use the Tk 3 lakh to pay recruitment debts [H0034]. A blinded worker got nothing after the case against his employer was dismissed [H0015]. The Bangladesh insurance does pay for natural death [H0049].
- Insurance: death money reportedly goes to the worker's bank account without an application; disability and job-loss must be claimed; very few claims (1,397 of 3.2 million workers since late 2019) [H0045]; one family received Tk 35,000 + Tk 3 lakh + Tk 10 lakh [H0048]. Reported exclusions (suicide/self-harm within 6 months, HIV/AIDS, war or riot, criminal attack, execution) [H0047, lead].

### 5. Coming home (feeds "Coming home")

- Official: PKB rehabilitation loan up to Tk 50 lakh, Tk 3 lakh without collateral, 9% [B0104-B0107]; airport desks, Wage Earners' Centre, Probashi Lounge [B0078-B0080, B0095-B0097].
- Practice: in 2021 only about 2% of pandemic returnees got PKB's reintegration loan; lack of staff and promotion, short grace period, 37% did not know it existed [H0030]; a returnee was put off by the conditions [H0031]. (2021 scheme differs from today's loan.)
- Crisis returns 2026: see section 2 [H0040, H0012, H0013].

---

### Draft SOPs

**SOP H1. Send money home through a licensed channel** (extends part B SOP 7)
- When to use it: every transfer from Qatar.
- Steps:
  1. Use a bank or an exchange house; MOI asks people to use only approved exchange companies and banks [H0027, lead]; bKash partners in Qatar: Gulf Exchange, Doha Exchange, Commercial Bank of Qatar, Western Union, MoneyGram [B0124].
  2. Give the receiver's bank account (yours or a relative's) [H0016] or bKash number and full registered name [B0125].
  3. Cost: on QAR 730 expect about QAR 20 fee plus a 4-5% exchange-rate margin (6.7-7.7% total); sending more at once costs less per riyal [B0122, B0123]. Ask for the rate and the fee before paying.
  4. Keep the receipt.
- What you see: money in the account within 3 days at most [H0017]; the family gets the 2.5% incentive [B0126] (not on money paid into an NRCTA [H0020] or used for bonds [H0025, lead]).
- Saving options: open an NRCTA before you leave (no initial deposit) [H0019] or buy a Wage Earner Development Bond (12% at maturity up to Tk 15 lakh, tax-free) [H0001, H0002, H0005].
- If it goes wrong: QCB consumer protection 44456412 / 44456456 [B0161]; bKash 16247 [B0173]. Never use hundi [B0013]; informal exchange is prosecuted in Qatar [H0026, lead].

**SOP H2. Claim death benefits from the Welfare Board and insurance** (family in Bangladesh)
- When: a worker with BMET clearance or Board membership dies in Qatar.
- Steps:
  1. Body home: employer pays; Embassy NOC; Board can fund [B0152, B0084, H0028].
  2. At the airport desk: Tk 35,000 cheque with passport copy, heirship certificate, heirs' NIDs, death certificate, NOC, airway bill [B0077].
  3. Tk 3 lakh grant: no separate application if the airport cheque was taken; if buried in Qatar apply within 1 year [B0072, B0074]; paid by BEFTN with SMS [B0075]. Practice: about 2 months [H0029].
  4. Insurance Tk 10 lakh, including natural death [H0049]; WEWB recommends to JBC [H0052]; reportedly automatic to the worker's bank account [H0045]. Insurance certificate from the JBC e-service [H0052].
  5. Qatari compensation (accident/workplace death only): power of attorney to the Embassy, lawyer, cheque through WEWB [B0146, B0151, B0086]. Practice: rare for "natural cause" deaths [H0032, H0033].
- If it goes wrong: Probash-Bondhu 16135 / +8809610102030 [B0069]; JBC Group Insurance 02-223381423, gid@jbc.gov.bd [H0053]; WEWB complaints [B0094].

**SOP H3. Claim for injury, disability or job loss**
- Treatment aid: up to Tk 2 lakh, apply within 1 year of return with medical reports, bank statement, arrival stamp and passport [B0081].
- Disability insurance: 100% total, 15-90% partial [H0050]; must be claimed through WEWB [H0045, H0052].
- Job loss: Tk 50,000 if back within 6 months, not if the employer paid your full migration cost [H0051]; claim through WEWB [B0060].
- **Need to know:** the insurance claim form and document list (WEWB/JBC pages searched).

**SOP H4. Get a Travel Permit (outpass)** (extends SOP L9)
- Lost passport: police GD first (QR 10) [L0184].
- Bring: form, old passport copy, 3 photos; attend in person [L0155]. The form asks for your parents' names, permanent address in Bangladesh, a reference person, old passport details [H0041]. Do not download it from `bdembassydoha.org` [H0042].
- Fee QR 50 / QR 98 urgent; Sun-Thu 8:30-4; valid 3 months [L0156, L0157].
- Then: clear status with MOI (surrender SOP L10) [L0176, L0120].

**SOP H5. Before you fly out of Qatar for good**
- Make sure all dues are paid before you sign anything; a signed discharge can block later claims [L0020, L0013, L0096, H0036].
- Know your RP cancellation date: MOI officers say 14 days to leave, QR 10/day after [H0037] (law says 90 days [L0111]: conflict).
- Open Metrash: pay overstay and traffic fines, check for a travel ban; if banned, go to the department that issued it [H0038, H0011 lead].
- Use the e-gates [H0039].
- If stopped from leaving: Exit Grievances Committee, 3 working days [L0103].

### Need to know (with what was searched)

1. **Official MOI source for the 14-day post-cancellation period** (searched MOI portal, QNA, Al Meezan law text, web; only Gulf Times/Gulf News/advisory firms).
2. **Official MOI/SJC travel-ban inquiry and lifting procedure** (only 2022 news of an SJC tweet; MOI services pages; web).
3. **Any 2026 grace period/amnesty** (MOI, QNA, Qatari press; none found).
4. **Current Travel Permit form location** (Embassy page link hijacked; doha.mofa.gov.bd pages).
5. **Qatar exchange houses with Bangladesh drawing arrangements** (Bangladesh Bank tool blocked) and **QCB licensed list** (part B: not found).
6. **Bangladesh Bank's current wage-remittance incentive circular** (BB circular list: captcha; Wayback copy of July 2026 list has no wage-remittance incentive circular in its last 3 months).
7. **Insurance claim forms, documents and the exclusions list** (WEWB insurance page, JBC leaflet; exclusions only in news [H0047]).
8. **What happens when an employer refuses to cancel the RP after the worker has left** (forums only; no official source).
9. **Qatar-specific hundi evidence** (English and Bangla news, research centres).
10. **Recent (2025-2026) evidence on gratuity being paid or withheld for Bangladeshis** (only 2019-2023 NGO reports; L0203-L0206).

### Conflicts

- **Days to leave after RP cancellation:** news of MOI officer statements, 14 days + QR 10/day [H0037, H0006, H0008, H0043] vs Law 21/2015 consolidated text, 90 days [L0111, S0034]. Law is the official rule; the news shows current practice at the airport. Also L0120: QR 12,000 settlement for not leaving after the RP ends vs QR 10/day in the news.
- **Wage Earner Development Bond:** Embassy Doha page (Nov 2021): Tk 50,000 minimum, up to Tk 1 crore in a lifetime, 12/11/10/9% by amount [B0155] vs National Savings Directorate (Aug 2026): Tk 25,000 minimum, no ceiling, 12/11/10/9% by slab [H0001, H0002, H0004]. Newer official wins.
- **Insurance:** part B's B0058/B0059 start-date conflict: the JBC leaflet says contract signed 12 March 2023, effective 1 January 2023 [H0049], which explains both dates.

### Blocked

See `blocked-parts/H.md`: Bangladesh Bank exchange-house search for Qatar; BB FE circular list (captcha) and incentive circular; MOI/SJC travel-ban pages; MOI 14-day release; Embassy Doha Facebook posts; current Travel Permit form; two news articles (Daily Star reintegration loans, Business Insider BD death compensation); PM Decision 34/2025.


## N: N

## Part N findings: practical pass, non-work visas (Hayya A1, family visit and residence, business, transit, student, medical, visit-visa scams)

Researched 7 Nov 2026. Ledger ids N0001-N0058 (ledger-parts/N.csv), 22 new sources. Status: 37 unofficial, 10 possibly-outdated (official, undated), 7 conflict, 4 lead. Official sources added: Hamad International Airport "Transfers" page [N0018-N0019]; Discover Qatar "Hayya E-Visa & Visa on Arrival - Approved Hotels" (Wayback, 1 Oct 2026) [N0011-N0014]; Visit Qatar health insurance page (Wayback, Apr 2023) [N0015]; HBKU scholarship guidelines [N0052-N0056]. Everything else is practice: Doha Guides (a private Doha expat guide whose pages were updated Nov 2025, plus reader comments), Daily Star, The Peninsula, Prothom Alo, New Nation, Bangladesh Monitor, QNA.

Method notes: Doha Guides is used only for what happens in practice; every exact fact from it is labelled "unofficial, not verified" and, where an official rule exists, set as a conflict with the official row as the rule. No Bangladesh-specific first-hand report of a Hayya A1 approval or refusal was found (searched English and Bangla web, Reddit, FlyerTalk, Doha Guides comments; visa-agency sites claiming a "78% approval rate" for Bangladeshis cite no source and were not used). Unused raws: `hia-transfers-wb` (duplicate of the curl copy), `expatcom-qatar-tourist-to-work-visa` (Canadian visa-on-arrival case, not relevant), `peninsula-ph-embassy-fake-evisa-site-2023` (Philippine e-visa, not Qatar). A FlyerTalk post (Feb 2023, Filipino transit traveller) was read via Firecrawl but not saved or used.

### Tourist visit: Hayya A1 in practice

- **Processing time:** officially no fixed time [V0019]. Readers report A1s stuck at "Pending" for 10 days to 3 weeks (2024) and the guide's only advice is to call the Hayya helpline +974 4441 2022 [N0005]; in Oct 2023 a "strict security check" caused about 3-week delays with no official announcement [N0006]. A wrong passport upload or name can be refused within hours, and a re-submission goes back into the queue [N0007].
- **Hotel booking:** Discover Qatar (Qatar Airways' destination company, an approved accommodation provider for Hayya) says booking through it is mandatory for "certain nationalities" applying for Hayya [N0011]; its visa-on-arrival list names only India, Pakistan, Thailand and Iran, with others possibly added [N0013]. Bangladesh is not named anywhere. Order of statuses: Pending, then Pending Accommodation Verification, then upload voucher, then Pending Payment [N0012] (matches Hayya interface V0132). Discover Qatar keeps QAR 100 if the visa is refused [N0014]. Practice: hotel proof is a PDF under 2 MB, a rejected proof can be replaced [N0009]; for "Host Family & Friends" the host does the steps in his own Hayya account before the visitor applies [N0010].
- **Return ticket:** within 30 days of entry, because the A1 is first issued for 30 days [N0002].
- **Health insurance in practice:** conflict. MoPH (mandatory for all visitors needing a prior visa, QAR 50/month [V0133, V0134]) and MOI [V0062] are the rule. Visit Qatar (2023 capture) said required for stays over 30 days, can be bought on arrival [N0015]; Doha Guides says "Recommended. Only mandatory if planning to stay for more than 30 days" [N0001]. Medical visitors: QNA (Oct 2023) reports public HMC/PHCC treatment fees apply to visitors first [N0016]; an international policy is accepted only if it meets MoPH standards and covers the whole stay [N0017] (lead).
- **Extension in practice:** one reader (Mar 2025) says buying 60 days of insurance extended the A1 automatically with no immigration visit, visible on MOI Visa Inquiry [N0003]; others (Nov 2024, Mar 2025) could not extend online [N0004]. Official route is still in conflict (V0025 vs V0060).

### Family visit and family residence (for Bangladeshi workers in Qatar)

- **Labour-category bar in practice:** labour-category jobs (drivers, delivery workers, cleaners) "may not be eligible ... irrespective of their salary" for family residence [N0038]; family visit may be refused for a labour-type profession on the QID, though an MOI Service Centre Captain can reverse it [N0047]. Official rule: E0058, E0069.
- **Salary:** checked through WPS salary transfers and the labour contract on file, not uploaded statements [N0039]. Doha Guides says QR 10,000 for other relatives' visit visas [N0048] (official gives QAR 5,000 only, E0069).
- **Restrictions (late 2025):** family residence and visit visas "temporarily restricted ... particularly for certain nationalities" with a backlog; nationalities not named [N0037]. Searched English/Bangla news for Bangladesh-specific restriction: none found.
- **Attestation:** marriage and children's birth certificates attested by the Qatar Embassy in the home country (or home embassy in Qatar) and then Qatar MOFA Doha [N0040]; for a spouse's visit visa, marriage-certificate attestation is now compulsory, children's birth certificates not [N0049]. Bangladesh Embassy Doha attests nikahnama QR 50 [E0043].
- **Application:** Metrash, JPG only, max 500 KB, names typed in Arabic [N0041]. No fixed time; CID clearance about a week once a visa number is issued [N0042]. Refusal: ask for a meeting with the visa approval committee at the Immigration Office [N0043]. Entry within 180 days (sometimes 90) [N0045]; family visit 90 days, extensions to 180 days for spouse/children after a QAR 100 medical [N0051]; insurance bought from an SMS link after approval [N0050].
- **Costs (conflict):** Qatar ID QR 500 first year (Doha Guides) [N0046] vs QR 400 (official, E0066). "No health insurance needed" on a family RP visa [N0044] vs MOI form requiring insurance (E0065).

### Business visit

- No practice reports for Bangladeshi business visitors found (searched English/Bangla news, Doha Guides). Official route only (V0085-V0093).

### Transit

- **Airside, no visa:** HIA says a transit visa is not needed if you do not leave the airport [N0018]; to leave, go to a transfer desk, not Arrivals [N0019]. Doha Guides agrees [N0020]. This closes V's Need to know on airside transit (official, undated).
- **Online transit visa:** Doha Guides replies (2023) say online transit visas suspended since 2020 and to ask the local Qatar Airways office [N0021] (conflict with Qatar Airways' online stopover visa V0104-V0105). Documents in practice: colour passport copy, 3 months' validity, 6x6 cm white photo, files under 1 MB [N0022].

### Student

- **HBKU (Hamad Bin Khalifa University, Education City) official:** scholarship considered automatically with admission, not guaranteed [N0052]; international stipends PhD QAR 9,000/month (45 months), Master's QAR 7,000/month (21 months) [N0053]; tuition waivers 100%/75%/60%, self-funded QAR 2,000 per credit hour [N0054]; housing paid by the student, one return air ticket [N0055]; max 20 hours' work a week [N0056].
- **Conflict:** Prothom Alo (Oct 2025) says HBKU provides housing and QAR 7,500 a month [N0057]; HBKU official is the rule [N0055, N0053].
- **Practice:** a significant number of Bangladeshi students graduated from Qatar University in May 2026 (embassy post via Bangladesh Monitor) [N0058]. QU scholarships themselves: S0020-S0024.

### Medical visit

- No separate medical visa found (V); visitors pay treatment fees at public hospitals and their insurance covers emergencies only [N0016, N0017; V0111-V0112].

### Visit visa to work: the trap

- Real Bangladeshi case: 23 men brought to Qatar on tourist visas for "lucrative jobs", Tk 3-3.5 lakh each, helped by dishonest recruiting agencies; jobless 3 months, visas expired, they became illegal; Qatari police arrested four brokers (Daily Star 2014) [N0023, N0024].
- Same pattern with Hayya: agents sold Hayya permits as "work visas" (Nigeria, 2023) [N0026]; Hayya CEO: not a work visa (V0128).
- "Free visa" practice (2026 op-ed): Tk 4-7 lakh, no job on arrival, illegal day labour, arrest and deportation risk [N0027]; a worker who paid Tk 4.5 lakh worked only two and a half of eight months (Daily Star 2018) [N0028].
- Bangladesh side: government said in 2012 it cannot bar a valid-visa holder but wanted screening of first-time tourist-visa travellers [N0025]. No current Ministry/BMET warning against visit visas for work found (see Blocked).

### Scams and checks (SOP material)

- Fake Qatar job adverts with fake offer letters, contracts "and even Qatar visa", Tk 3-4 lakh each, ~1,000 victims (2019) [N0029].
- Fake government websites: genuine Qatari government sites end in gov.qa; look-alikes like gov-qa.com are phishing (NCSA via Peninsula, Mar 2026) [N0030]. Hayya's own address is hayya.qa (V0005, V0061), so the SOP must name hayya.qa and the "Hayya to Qatar" app explicitly.
- Bangladesh Embassy Doha warned (14 Jan 2026) against people in Qatar taking money to move migrants on to Europe or America [N0031] (lead; embassy post blocked).
- Ami Probashi (private platform) paid visa check for Qatar on 16768, SMS valid/invalid [N0032] (unofficial; not an official check).

### Draft SOPs

#### SOP N-1 (add to SOP V-2): Check a Qatar visa you were given is real
- **When:** anyone hands you a Qatar visa, Hayya e-visa or visa copy, or says it is "approved".
- **Steps:** (1) Hayya e-visa: hayya.qa > Track your E-Visa (Entry Reference Number, or passport number + nationality Bangladesh) [V0054]. (2) Any MOI visa: portal.moi.gov.qa > Inquiries > Visa Services > Visa Inquiry and Printing (fees2.moi.gov.qa/moipay/visa/inquiry), enter the visa number (or passport number + nationality), type the captcha, Submit [V0057, V0059; practice N0033]. Free. (3) Type the address yourself; government sites end in gov.qa, Hayya is hayya.qa [N0030, V0005].
- **What you see (unofficial, not verified):** name, nationality, passport number, visa type, stay duration, issue date, validity, status, sponsor [N0034]. Status "Valid To Use" = issued, can travel; "Ready To Print" = approved, sponsor must pay; "Under Process"/"Under Outside Process" = not finished; "Rejected", "Visa Expired", "Cancelled"; "Sorry, Requested Visa Not Found" = not processed or wrong details [N0035, N0036]. Check the visa TYPE: a tourist/visit/Hayya visa never allows work [V0024].
- **If it goes wrong:** do not pay or travel; Hayya +974 4441 2022 [V0030]; MOI 2367111 [V0138]; in Bangladesh, police (the 2019 gang was reported at Tejgaon police station [N0029]).

#### SOP N-2: Bring family on a visit visa (worker in Qatar)
- **When:** you are in Qatar, not in a labour-category job, earning QAR 5,000+ [E0069; practice N0047].
- **Steps:** attest the nikahnama (Qatar Embassy Dhaka or Bangladesh Embassy Doha, then Qatar MOFA) [N0040, N0049, E0043]; Metrash > Visa > Family Visit Visa; upload passport (6 months' validity), relationship proof, accommodation [E0068]; pay QR 200 [E0067]; buy insurance from the SMS link [N0050]; family enters within the "visa valid till" date [N0051].
- **If refused:** reason shown in Metrash; ask the MOI Service Centre Captain or the visa approval committee [N0047, N0043] (unofficial).

#### SOP N-3: Transit through Doha
- Stay airside: no visa [N0018]. To leave: transfer desk; QAR 100 transit visa via Discover Qatar desk (under 12 h) or stopover booking (12-96 h) [V0104, N0019].

### Need to know (with what was searched)
- Any Bangladesh-specific Hayya A1 refusal or approval data (searched English/Bangla news, Reddit, FlyerTalk, Doha Guides comments; none).
- Whether Bangladeshis must book Hayya hotels through Discover Qatar ("certain nationalities" not named) [N0011].
- Which nationalities faced the 2025 family visa restriction [N0037].
- Official Bangladesh warning against going to Qatar on visit visas for work (probashi.gov.bd, bmet.gov.bd notices, web; only 2012 news N0025).
- Official MOI visa-inquiry result wording (needs a real visa number).
- Bangladesh MOFA attestation/e-Apostille route for nikahnama to be used in Qatar (Qatar is not an Apostille party per HCCH; Bangladesh joined 30 Mar 2025, raw hcch-apostille-status); fee and steps not found on an official page.
- Business-visitor practice for Bangladeshis; Qatar Embassy Dhaka visit-visa service (blocked in V).

### Conflicts
- Visitor insurance: MoPH/MOI mandatory for all prior-visa visitors [V0133, V0062] (rule) vs Visit Qatar 2023 "over 30 days" [N0015] vs Doha Guides "only over 30 days" [N0001].
- Online transit visa: Qatar Airways online stopover visa [V0104-V0105] (rule) vs Doha Guides "suspended since 2020" [N0021].
- Family RP: Qatar ID QR 400 official [E0066] vs QR 500 [N0046]; health insurance required by MOI form [E0065] vs "not required" [N0044].
- HBKU: no housing paid, stipend 7,000/9,000 [N0055, N0053] (rule) vs Prothom Alo housing and QAR 7,500 [N0057].
- Family visit salary for other relatives: official QAR 5,000 [E0069] vs Doha Guides QR 10,000 [N0048] (noted, row unofficial).

### Blocked
See blocked-parts/N.md: Bangladesh Embassy Doha 14 Jan 2026 advisory; NCSA fake-website infographic; current Visit Qatar insurance page; sample MOI visa-inquiry result; any Ministry/BMET visit-visa warning; Qatar Airways transit portal (also V).


## R: R

## Findings, part R (re-check of every `lead` and `conflict` row)

Researcher R, 7 November 2026. Scope: the 58 `lead` and 30 `conflict` rows in `ledger.csv` (88 rows). New evidence is in `ledger-parts/R.csv` (R0001-R0028). Where an official row already existed in another part (notably the user's QVC pages and the Ministry's 18 May 2026 Facebook post, part M), that row is cited instead of being copied.

Main new official sources:
- Al Meezan (Ministry of Justice legal portal API): MOI Decision 25/2019 (residence regulations, in force), MOI Decision 51/2020 (change of employer), MOI Decision 92/2019 (temporary work visa), Law 22/2021 (health care), MoPH Decision 8/2022 (its regulations) and MoPH Decisions 17/2022 and 3/2023 (visitor premium).
- MOI's live "Visit Visa Extension" form page.
- Hukoomi page "Apply to Replace Employer (Before Getting Residency Permit)" (2021, old rules).
- NHRC home page (Nov 2026), GCO labour-reform page (Mar 2026), MOL 2024 trafficking report (archived).
- Bangladesh budget speech 2026-27 (Finance Division, 11 June 2026).
- Jusour's Sahem programme page.

### Settled rules (what the doc should say)

- **Welfare Board fee paid from Qatar:** the rule is Tk 6,500 equivalent in local currency, life insurance included [B0065, WEWB page, 7 Oct 2026]. It matches the Ministry circular raising the welfare fee to Tk 5,500 from 1 Oct 2026 for people going abroad, people already abroad and payments at the airport [B0055]. The Embassy's QR 140 [B0138/L0167, Nov 2025] and QR 170 [B0141/L0183, 2021] predate the increase. No newer Embassy notice giving the QAR amount was found (Embassy notices and press-release lists were checked). **Need to know:** the QAR amount the Embassy counter now charges.
- **Welfare fee Tk 3,500 vs Tk 5,500:** the circular [B0055] is the rule from 1 Oct 2026; the BMET charter [B0051] predates it.
- **Change of employer:** the law is the rule. MOI Decision 51/2020 replaced Art 65 of the regulations: the worker moves under the labour rules by notifying the labour ministry, with the residence permit valid or within 90 days of expiry [R0001, R0002]. It repealed Art 66, the old "end of contract or five years" limit [R0003]. Before the residence permit is issued, a work visa can be moved to another employer with labour-ministry approval [R0028]. This adds to Decree-Law 19/2020 [L0122] and GCO [L0210]. The old MOI page (two years plus an NOC) [L0146] and the 2021 Hukoomi page asking for an NOC [R0004] are outdated.
- **Health insurance:** the rule is that insurance is compulsory for residents and visitors (Law 22/2021 Art 8) [R0013]. No visa, residence permit or renewal is issued without proof of cover [R0014]. The visitor premium is QR 50 a month, and the same applies on extension [R0015]. Visitor cover must run from the date of entry, and international insurance that includes Qatar is accepted [R0016]. Visit Qatar's "recommend" [V0006] is weaker than the law. MOI [V0062] and MoPH [V0133] agree with the law. **Need to know:** whether the residents' phase is enforced in practice. No MoPH notice on it was reached; the MoPH scheme page is behind a captcha.
- **Passport validity:** these are different rules for different visas, not one conflict.
  - **Law default:** for any non-residence visa, the passport must be valid six months from the date of the visa, and the authority may waive this [R0005, R0006]. This is the MOI rule [V0096].
  - **Hayya e-visas:** Hayya applies 3 months from arrival [V0011, V0056].
  - **Work visas at QVC:** QVC books appointments only for passports valid more than 195 days [M0016, M0017].
- **Business visa length:** the regulations are the rule [R0007, Art 30]. Tourist and business visit visas are single entry for 30 or 60 days, or multiple entry for 6 months or 1 year with at most 90 days per trip. The businessmen visa runs through licensed companies or a service provider [R0008]. The MOI service pages (1 month [V0085]; 90 days [V0093]; 1-6 months under a 2007 decision [V0072]) are older product descriptions.
- **Transit visa:** 4 to 96 hours, single trip (Regulations Art 44) [R0010]. This matches Qatar Airways [V0104]. The old MOI page (12-48 hours, or 4-12 hours) [V0092] is outdated.
- **GCC-resident visa:** the regulations require a profession on the approved list [R0009]. This supports MOI [V0073]. Hayya's "others" job-title option [V0033] is a form field. Not relevant to Bangladeshis applying from Bangladesh.
- **QVC centres in Bangladesh:** QVC's own Bangladesh page lists Dhaka and Sylhet [M0013], and its updates page says Sylhet takes appointments [M0014]. The Ministry's 18 May 2026 post says there is only one visa and medical centre, in Dhaka [M0063], and news says the same [W0009]. QVC is the authority on its own centres, and its page was saved on 7 Nov 2026 and lists 2026 holidays, so the doc should show Dhaka and Sylhet. Show the Ministry statement as a conflict.
- **Newborn residence permit:** the law sets 90 days [S0035]. The 60-day figure [S0133] matches a different rule: the visit visa for a baby born in Qatar to a mother on a visit visa must be applied for within 60 days [R0025].
- **In-country change of visa purpose:** the 2019 regulations allow it at the authority's discretion [S0038]. They replace the 2015 law-firm alert [S0131].
- **Temporary work visa:** the regulation says up to six months [R0012]. Gulf Times 2024 says up to 12 months [W0095]. This is a new official-vs-news conflict, and the official row is the rule.
- **Treatment aid and ambulance:** the newer WEWB pages [B0081 up to Tk 2 lakh; B0080 free] replace the 2024 annual report [B0091, B0092].

### Conflicts that stand

- **Police clearance fee:** the PCC portal says Tk 1,500, code 1-7301-0001-2681 [B0043]. The police.gov.bd guidance [B0047] and the Embassy page [B0150, 2021] say Tk 500, code 1-2201-0001-2681. No fee notification was found (searched in English and Bangla for the fee and the new code). The portal is the live payment system, so show Tk 1,500 as what applicants are asked for and Tk 500 as older guidance.
- **Visit-visa extension:** MOI's live online "Visit Visa Extension" service exists. It takes a visa or passport number, the nationality, a captcha, then "Extend" [R0017, R0018, V0060]. The Hayya FAQ says extensions are processed at the Directorate of Passport at Hamad airport [V0025]. Both are official, so show both.
- **Insurance start date:** 1 Jan 2023 [B0058] vs 12 Mar 2023 [B0059]. Both give 5 years and Tk 10 lakh, so there is no practical effect for people leaving now.
- **Qatar-Oman joint visa list:** 6 countries [V0069] vs about 37 [V0009]. Bangladesh is on neither list.
- **72-hour business visa on arrival:** its page lists no nationalities [V0088], while the special on-arrival page ties it to Iran [V0084]. The 2019 regulations list no 72-hour type. Not open to Bangladeshis either way.
